# T. rex tail saturation habit: everything tried

**Date:** 2026-10-29 (the work of 2026-07-09 to 2026-10-29); updated
2026-10-30 (§9: the work of 2026-10-29 23:47 UTC to 2026-10-30 16:32 UTC)

**Status:** dated record, in the repository from the records pull request of
decision D-D55 (3) (branch `claude/records-tail-habit`; its number is in its
CHANGELOG entry and in the consolidation plan's status table). The maintainer
asked for it at 18:18 UTC on 2026-10-29: "There a lot of good lesson learned,
like with the foot problems, just want to make sure we capture everything we
have tried" (RUNLOG l753, D-D55 (1)); and chose at 23:21 UTC to keep it as its
own note with its evidence, "Own note + evidence PR" (RUNLOG l759, D-D55 (3)).
It gathers in one place what the records hold about the T. rex tail saturation
habit, the way
[TREX_STANCE_SYMMETRY_AND_FOOT_2026_10.md](TREX_STANCE_SYMMETRY_AND_FOOT_2026_10.md)
and [../NEXT_STEPS.md](../NEXT_STEPS.md) §6 gather the stance's foot problems
and the program's lessons.

## How to read this record

- **Labels.** [measured] means read or computed from the named file, by whoever
  that file names. [inferred] means reasoned from measured numbers, with the
  basis named. A number is given as its source prints it. Where two sources
  print the same quantity differently, both are given with their sources.
- **Links and paths.** Repository links are relative to
  `docs/investigations/`: the files of `main` at `378ae2f8` and the evidence
  added with this note. A line number cited in a repository file is that
  file's line at `378ae2f8`; the same change adds lines to `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`,
  `BEHAVIOR_RECIPES_PLAN.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, the consolidation plan and the
  stance record, which moves some of them (each file as of commit `378ae2f8`
  gives the cited text). A path in code font that starts with `scratchpad/` or
  `scratchpad2/` is a file of the session's scratch that is not kept in the
  repository; `scratchpad/` is the first session's scratch root and
  `scratchpad2/` the second's, as the evidence folder's `SOURCES.txt` names
  them (full paths in §8.2), and §8.2 says where each one's content is found
  instead.
- **The run log.** "RUNLOG lN" means line N of the session run log
  `scratchpad/foot/stance4/live/run_log.txt`, which had 753 lines when the
  draft was written, 757 when the update read it and 759 when the change that
  adds this note copied it. Every line of it is in a repository copy, with the
  session's tool ids removed; §8.2 maps its line ranges to the copies. Lines
  721 to 759 (2026-10-28 21:05 UTC to 2026-10-29 23:21 UTC) are
  [stance_2026_10/ecs_results/run_log.txt](stance_2026_10/ecs_results/run_log.txt)
  (copy line = N - 720).
- **Steps, seeds and windows.** In screen labels M = 1,048,576 steps (4.19M =
  4,194,304). "s42" is a training seed. The deterministic panels use reset
  seeds 3042-3061 (20 episodes) or 3042-3081 (40). The window is steps 200-999
  of the 1,000-step stance episode.
- **Runs.**
  - stance3 Run 2 is `stance3_armLT_s60` (arm L+T).
  - stance4 s42 is `stance4_LTS_s42`.
  - stance5 s42 is `stance5_LTSB_s42` (the per-foot CoP-band arm, LTSB).
  - stance5r s80 is `stance5_LTSB_s80`, the band's replication.
  - The r10 runs are `stance6_r10LTSB_s60`, `_s42` and `_s80`.
  - The entropy continuations are named `s42_A_1101` and so on.
- **Plants.** T. rex physics r6 and r7 are the plants of the August stage-1
  runs. r8 and r9 are the October plants: r9 is `main`'s plant for run A and
  the stance3 to stance5r screens. r10 is SR3, the preloaded tail springs of
  D-D44, on `main` from 2026-10-25.
- **Short names used in the tables.** Each stands for the linked file. §8.1
  and §8.2 list every file this record cites.

## 1. What the habit is and how it is detected

### 1.1 The habit

In deterministic play a trained T. rex stance policy drives one actuator's
command to its bound and keeps it there. Almost always the actuator is
`tail_3_pitch_act`, the distal tail pitch servo (action index 14 of 15),
commanded to +1, tail tip up. That was the actuator and sign in every railed
r9 run that was named (stance3 Run 2, stance4 s42, stance5 s42 and stance5r
s80) and in the railed r10 run, s42 [measured; L027, L032, L045, L054, L100].

- **What saturates is the command, not the joint or the motor** [measured].
  - On r9, at stance4 s42's 4,194,304 checkpoint, the joint sat at -0.007
    rad. That is 0.216 rad below its +0.2094 rad stop, carrying 0.26 of its
    50 N.m limit (SATDIAG §1) [L035].
  - On r10, command +1 holds the joint at +5.19°, with +7.1 N.m of servo
    torque against ±50 N.m, "not saturated" (VCLAMP `static_tail.txt` l1-8,
    VCLAMP `NOTES.txt` l76; CLAMP `NOTES.txt` l56-80) [L138]. [inferred, this
    record] That is about 43% of the joint's 12° upward range (5.19 of 12°;
    the servo-spring split kp/(kp+k) = 0.4286, `static_tail.txt` l8).
- **The servo.** tail_3 is a position servo with kp 60, control range ±0.2094
  rad (±12°) and force range ±50 N.m, plus a passive spring of stiffness 80
  N.m/rad [measured; S3SAT §2 line 37, CLAMP `NOTES.txt` l56-80].
  - The spring rested at 0 through r9.
  - On r10 it is preloaded to `springref` 10.4° (0.18151 rad) (PV note 20)
    [L090].
- **How far the command moves the joint.**
  - On the r9 statue the whole command range moves the joint only q3 =
    -0.0924 + 0.0863a rad, so a straight distal joint would need a = +1.073
    (BRIEF §1 item 5) [L061].
  - On r10, a = 0 holds +0.014° and a = +1 holds +5.19° [L138].
- **What it costs** [measured].
  - It fails the gate's saturation key in deterministic panels, without
    falls [L009, L032, L054].
  - It costs reward, from +73.1 to +85.9 per window on r9 (SATDIAG §4) to
    about 80-100 per episode directly on r10 (COMREV §4) [L039, L140].
  - It costs no balance: a stance with the rail removed stands the same
    (COMREV §4 (i)) [L141].
- **The same habit on other actuators.**
  - r6: 10-12 of 21 actuators pinned (tail, neck, head, toes) [L004].
  - r7: knees, head_pitch and the tail yaw/pitch chain [L011].
  - r9 stance3 Run 3, a run with no alarm: `neck_pitch` at a window mean of
    -0.700 and `head_pitch` -0.298 at 4,194,304, with no window step at 0.9;
    Run 2 held `head_pitch` at -0.52 beside its tail rail (S3SAT §1 lines
    31-34) [L155].
  - r9 stance5r s70, a run that held and raised no alarm: its final held
    `tail_2_pitch_act` at a window-mean command of +0.767 with 40/40 clean,
    and the same policy pinned tail_2 at +1 on r10 zero-shot. It is
    "PARKED-like at R2"; its R1 and J commands were never measured (EVID6
    lines 137-138, 216-217, 402-403) [L154].
  - r10 s80: `neck_pitch_act` at -1, then `head_pitch_act` parked negative
    [L101, L102, L106].
  - The entropy screen's s80 continuations: the neck again [L123].

**The history in brief** [measured].

- **August.** The tail was first seen saturated among many actuators on r6
  and r7 [L004, L010, L011].
- **The #504 fix.** The shaping pack #504 priced saturation and tail pose.
  The r7 gate-pass run and its replicates then saturated nothing [L013-L015],
  and KNOWN_ISSUES deleted the item as fixed on 2026-08-15 [L016].
- **Back on r9.** From the stance3 screen of 2026-10-18/19 the habit
  returned, as one actuator and one sign [L027]. It raised an alarm in 4 of
  10 r9 L+T-family runs [L064]; a fifth ended with tail_2 at +0.767 and no
  flag [L154].
- **The tail-habit round.** Training-loss bounds on the mean failed or masked
  it [L049, L078].
- **Physics r10.** The new plant was built so a level tail costs no command
  [L088, L090]. The first r10 round still formed a rail [L100].
- **2026-10-29.** Removing the remaining entropy bonus changed nothing from
  mid-rise states [L120]. A clamp test then measured the persistence
  mechanism, a learned positive-feedback loop [L145].
- **2026-10-29, evening.** A normaliser probe placed the loop's growth in the
  policy's weights, not in the drift of its observation normaliser, which at
  fixed weights works against it [L158].
  Posture tests found that the s42 lineage's networks read their nose-down
  posture as a tail offset, which the loop amplifies, from mid-rise on. The
  plant gives no mechanical push, and in r10 s42 the tail bias rose before
  any posture shift [L162-L164].

### 1.2 How it is detected

The detectors appear in the order they were built. Each row says what it
catches and what it cannot see.

| Detector [ledger] | What it reads | Catches | Misses or limits |
|---|---|---|---|
| Training diagnostics `diagnostics/raw_action_saturation` and `diagnostics/action_saturation`, 2026-07-09 and 2026-08-15 [L001, L017] | Pre-clip share at \|a\| >= 0.99, and the env's 0.9 ramp share, pooled over actuators | That the mean leaves the bound (0.32 -> 0.68 in July [L002]) | Which actuator. Pooled. |
| Gate key `max_actuator_saturation_fraction`, `stance_quality/v2`, D-D23 of 2026-10-06 [L019] | The most saturated actuator's share of window steps at \|a\| >= 0.99 (`SATURATION_ABS` 0.99, clipped commands). T. rex bar 0.10. An episode over it counts in the hop-or-fall rail (at most 1 of 40 per panel, D-D27) | Rails in the judged deterministic panels | It names no actuator (until #640). It cannot see a parked command under 0.99 [L044]. It cannot see a rail that forms after the judged checkpoint: the notebook's gate judged early robust_best pairs [L031, L108]. It reads raw normalised commands, not force (PILOTS §2 l209-210) [L023], so it is also blind to real force saturation, as recorded for the Compsognathus MTP servo (KI lines 2064-2065; another species). |
| Reward ramp `action_saturation` 0.5 above 0.9 (#504) [L013], priced on the raw clipped command since D-D27 (`action_penalty_source = "raw"`) [L022] | A price, not a detector | | Policies park just under the ramp: head_pitch -0.890 [L014]; stance3 Run 2's final +0.841 [L028]. |
| Force columns `actuator_force_frac_max` and `actuator_force_limited_fraction` [L043] | Actuator force | Not this habit | 0.272 before and 0.283 during stance4 s42's rail, held by head_pitch or tail_1. The limited fraction is 0 in every s42 episode (SATDIAG §1). |
| The ledger alarm, proposed 2026-10-19 (STANCE §17.6), D-D39, report only [L030] | `eval_episodes.csv`: 10 deterministic episodes per evaluation, every 131,072 steps. An evaluation is flagged when at least 5 of its 10 episodes list `max_actuator_saturation_fraction` in `screen_v2_reasons`. Two flagged evaluations in a row are an alarm. | The whole training history, not only judged checkpoints | Until #640 it could not name the actuator, so naming it took offline rollouts (SATDIAG; `satwatch5.py --roll` [L046]). |
| Per-episode actuator naming, #640 of 2026-10-24 [L058] | Ledger columns `saturated_actuator`, `saturated_actuator_fraction` (the gate key, bit for bit), `saturated_actuator_positive_share` (sign), `saturated_actuator_ties` and `actuators_saturated` | Actuator and sign on every evaluation row | On r10 it equalled the readouts row for row (STANCE §27.3 D-1). |
| Training-rollout clip shares in `policy_probe.npz`, #640 [L059] | Per actuator: the share of sampled actions beyond each bound, at \|clip(a)\| >= 0.99, and the share of Gaussian means beyond each bound | The training-row "drive" (the r10 round's M6 readout) [L101] | The probe's 10-state bank is not an early detector [L070]. Since #630 the probe also stores the deterministic action on that bank under the current and the session-start normalisation (CL lines 1155-1164); it was never read for the normaliser question (§4.8). |
| Per-step trace tool, #638 of 2026-10-24 [L060] | Per step and actuator: raw mean, command, `data.ctrl`, joint angle and velocity, force; `--hold NAME=VALUE`, `--stochastic` | In-episode ramps, holds and counterfactuals (D-9/D-10, the clamp) | Offline. One checkpoint at a time. |
| Commands per actuator in the ledger, #644/#645 of 2026-10-25/26 [L095] | Window-mean command per actuator; at-rail and parked actuators named with their signs; tail angles and tip | A parked command (TEL `parked_actuators`) | Report only. |
| Observation-normalisation page `18_obs_normalization`, #645, the maintainer's telemetry item (4) of 2026-10-25 15:44 UTC [L095] | VecNormalize's statistics per checkpoint from the saved pickles, read through a stub unpickler that runs no library code (CL lines 263-284; RUNLOG l611, l616) | Which observation dimension drifts most between checkpoints: the source of the r10 readout D-15 (READOUTS line 625) [L104] | Report only. It cannot say whether a drift causes the posture or follows it [L104]. |
| Training-history report, #636/#639 [L057] | Pages drawn from the ledgers at every stage end; flagged episodes by actuator and sign; alarms marked | The habit at a glance, run by run | Report only. |

### 1.3 Glossary

- **Rail.** A command held at its bound. The records use the word in three
  related senses:
  - The gate's "saturation rail": its key over the 0.10 bar.
  - In the r10 criteria, an episode is over the **BAR** when its share of
    window steps at \|command\| >= 0.99 on an actuator exceeds 0.10 (CRIT6
    line 166). A run's tail class is **RAIL** when it has a TAIL ALARM or a
    TRACE RAIL (CRIT6 lines 269-272). A TRACE RAIL is at least 2
    full-horizon TAIL FLAGS on one actuator and sign among R1's and R2's 40
    traced episodes, or 1 on an actuator whose MEAN_a in that sign is >= 0.60
    (lines 188-191). A TRACE RAIL is printed **DRIVE-BACKED** when, at a
    checkpoint holding one of its flags, CMD_a is >= 0.60 in the flag's sign
    (lines 190-191). A RAIL run's kind is **RAIL-LEDGER-PERSISTENT** (a TAIL
    ALARM and a TAIL FLAG at R1 or R2), **RAIL-LEDGER-RESOLVED** (a TAIL ALARM,
    no flag at R1 or R2) or **RAIL-END-ONLY** (a TRACE RAIL without a TAIL
    ALARM) (lines 270-272).
  - In the D-D43 bound screen, a replicate is RAIL when an episode has more
    than 0.10 of its window steps at tail_3 >= +0.99 (STANCE §24.4 lines
    3799-3800) [L078].
- **Parked.** Two meanings, by period:
  - In August, "saturation parking" meant a command at or within 2% of a
    stop (NARROW §1 item 3) [L011].
  - From D-D43 (2026-10-24), **PARKED** means held high but under the rail:
    a window-mean command of 0.60 or more with the rail clear. It counts as
    NOT ADOPTED in a screen (STANCE §24.3) and as not a success in a round
    (D-D49 D6-D8: STANCE §26.4 lines 4550-4551; CRIT6 line 285) [L069,
    L096].
  - In the r10 criteria, a run is PARKED when, for a tail pitch actuator a,
    MEAN_a >= +0.60, or CMD_a(J) >= +0.60 at 2 or more of the J checkpoints
    (CRIT6 lines 276-277). The tail pitch actuators are 11, 13 and 14
    (`tail_1_pitch`, `tail_2_pitch`, `tail_3_pitch`), and TAIL-UP is a
    positive command on one of them (CRIT6 line 165). The rule reads all
    three, not tail_3 alone, because stance5r s70's r9 final held tail_2 at
    +0.767 and the same policy pinned it at +1 on r10 (EVID6 lines 137-138;
    DVREV DV3) [L154].
  - **DRIVEN** (report only, r10 criteria): any tail actuator with
    \|MEAN_a\| >= 0.60 that PARKED does not count, such as a tail driven down
    (CRIT6 line 279) [L089].
  - The 0.60 line came from the r9 parked states. Every saturated checkpoint
    of the affected runs sat at +0.87 to +1.0, and the unaffected runs never
    rose above +0.34 (SATDIAG §7 T2) [L044]. Both figures are tail_3
    commands of satdiag's runs; s70's tail_2 at +0.767 was measured later, in
    the SR3 zero-shot check [L089, L154].
  - **Limits of the line.** A run within about 0.05 of 0.60 classifies by
    chance: r10 s60's MEAN_a margin was +0.042 (its J window fired on its own,
    margins +0.28 and +0.13), and s80's final +0.588 sat inside the band
    below the line (REVIEW lines 162-164, 754-758). In the D-D43 screen the
    band was about 0.03, and BOUND60's b sat on the line (SCREEN
    `criteria.txt` line 421) [L044, L082, L100].
- **ANY flag, ANY alarm.** An ANY-FLAGGED EVALUATION has at least 5 of its 10
  ledger episodes over the bar on any actuator. An ANY ALARM is the second of
  two consecutive any-flagged evaluations: D-D40 5a, as r9 was read (CRIT6
  lines 169-170).
- **TAIL flag, TAIL alarm.**
  - A TAIL-FLAGGED EVALUATION has at least 5 of its 10 episodes over the bar
    with a tail actuator (indices 11-14) named. A TAIL ALARM is the second of
    two consecutive tail-flagged evaluations (CRIT6 lines 171-172).
  - In the traces, a TAIL FLAG (M1) is a tail actuator over the bar in a
    full-horizon traced episode, with its sign (CRIT6 lines 167-168).
- **CMD.**
  - In the r10 criteria, CMD_a(X) is the mean, over those of the 20 traced
    episodes at checkpoint X that last beyond step 200, of each episode's
    window-mean command of tail actuator a (CRIT6 lines 182-184).
  - In the D-D43 screen and the entropy screen, "CMD" or "command" is the
    same panel mean for tail_3.
- **MEAN_a.** (CMD_a(R1) + CMD_a(R2)) / 2, where R1 is the 10,485,760
  checkpoint and R2 the final. When only one of the two is formed, MEAN_a is
  that one (CRIT6 lines 181-187). In the D-D43 screen, R1 is 5,111,808 and R2
  the end (STANCE §24.3).
- **frac99.** The share of window steps at \|command\| >= 0.99 for tail_3
  (TAILEXP).
- **Drive.** The share of a training rollout's rows whose Gaussian mean lies
  beyond the bound. The r10 readout D-2 used 5% as its threshold (STANCE
  §27.3).
- **Statue.** The zero-action policy.
- **J checkpoints.** The six judged checkpoints of a screen (4,194,304,
  5,242,880, 6,291,456, 8,388,608, 10,485,760 and the final).
- **K1-K4.** The r9 screens' per-run criteria
  ([stance_2026_10/stance5/criteria.txt](stance_2026_10/stance5/criteria.txt)
  lines 68-79):
  - K1, "gate held": a v2 PASS at 6,291,456 and at the final, and across J at
    most one checkpoint below 37/40 clean and none below 33/40. This is where
    the rail fails a run.
  - K2: fore-aft held.
  - K3: falls under joint-space and own-sigma noise.
  - K4: sole roll.

## 2. Where it stands (2026-10-29)

**Known** [measured unless said]

- **On r9 and r10 it is a command habit of one actuator at a time** (on r6
  and r7 many actuators were pinned at once [L004, L011]). It is not a
  saturated joint, motor or force, so force-based telemetry cannot see it [L035, L043,
  L138].
- **It recurs on both plants.**
  - On r9 it raised an alarm in 4 of 10 L+T-family runs, every onset before
    4.5M (BRIEF §1 item 4) [L064]. That count is alarm-based. A fifth run,
    stance5r s70, held tail_2 at +0.767 at its final with no flag; and every
    other non-alarm run but stance4 s60 had saturated ledger episodes with no
    actuator named, so under r10's RAIL rule r9 reads 4 to 6 of 10 (DVREV
    DV5, inferred there) [L154].
  - On r10, in the first round of three runs: s42 railed tail_3 at +1, s60
    parked it (MEAN_a +0.642) and s80 formed a neck habit instead (STANCE
    §27.2) [L100, L106].
  - From s42's r10 3,145,728 checkpoint the rail formed in all 12 CPU
    continuations. It did not form on the tail in any of the 12 from s80's
    4,194,304 checkpoint (ECS `score/score.txt` l53-110) [L120, L123].
- **It costs reward, and the policy keeps paying.** Holding tail_3 at 0
  gains:
  - +85.9 and +73.1 per window on r9 stance4 s42, deterministic (SATDIAG §4).
  - +54.6 and +36.2 per window under the policy's own noise at the r9 4.19M
    checkpoints (STANCE §24.2).
  - +78.29 at r10 s42's onset (STANCE §27.3 D-9).
  - +63.6 per episode at the 12 railed entropy-screen ends (COMREV §4)
    [L039, L071, L103, L141].
  - The one exception is the r10 s42 final, which carried a parked command
    and no rail. There the hold loses 64.46 [L103].
- **It persists through a learned positive-feedback loop** [measured;
  mechanism inferred].
  - The tail_3 command rises with the tail_3 angle the policy observes. In
    the 12 railed ends the loop gain is +0.630 (median; range +0.480 to
    +0.910), against +0.268 at their start checkpoint. The loop's fixed point
    lies past the +1 bound, so the command parks at the rail. The 12 unrailed
    s80 ends push back (median -0.255) (VCLAMP `loop_summary.txt` l1-49)
    [L145].
  - Earlier estimates by other methods agree in sign: satdiag's observation
    swap, the unclipped probe and the hold cells (§4.7) [L037, L072, L073].
- **The loop's growth is in the weights, not in the normaliser** [measured,
  verified; L157-L159].
  - The tail_3 angle's normaliser scale fell to 0.437 of its start value as
    the tail rose. At fixed weights that lowers the gain: the start weights
    with the ends' statistics 0.268 -> 0.141, the end weights with the
    start's 0.630 -> 1.143 (12/12 each; the sign holds in every sweep range).
  - The growth sits downstream of the first layer, in the second hidden
    layer and the output layer. Per normalised unit the network's tail_3
    sensitivity grew x4.3-4.65 on matched ranges.
  - [inferred, PROBE §6] The drift is a record of the rail: the frames added
    between checkpoints sat at +2.77 to +3.89°, against the start's running
    mean of +0.47°.
- **From mid-rise on, the s42 networks read their posture as a tail
  offset** [measured, verified with corrections; L162-L164].
  - The s42 ends raise the tail command when they see the pelvis more
    nose-down (-0.157 per degree of nose-up, 0/12 positive) and their own
    knee and hip angles. The s80 ends' typical posture inputs remove almost
    the whole offset (a lineage-specific part of about -0.38, of about 0.40)
    and leave the gain unchanged.
  - Shown a pelvis 1.1° more nose-up, the s42 ends rail in 0.154 of their
    episodes instead of 0.671 (closed loop).
  - The plant plays no part: the tail map and the cost of tail-up are the
    same in the s42, s80 and statue postures [L163].
  - It is not where the habit started. In r10 s42 the tail bias rose first,
    at about 1.20M (at a fixed observation it was rising by 1.3-1.6M); the
    nose-down pitch followed about 0.17M later. At 1,048,576 the network had no
    posture-to-tail pathway and a negative loop (G -0.250); both grew
    between 2.1M and 3.15M. s60, which parked, has the opposite pitch
    sensitivity [L161, L164].
- **It forms in training from clean, mid-rise states.** All three D-D43
  CONTROL replicates from stance5r s80's clean 4,194,304 state read RAIL
  (STANCE §24.5) [L081]. All twelve r10 s42 continuations railed (ECS)
  [L120].

**Ruled out or narrowed** (each within the scope tested)

- **The plant forcing the pin.**
  - The r9 plant enabled the rail but did not force it (BRIEF §1 item 5).
  - r10, where a level tail costs no command, still railed [L061, L088,
    L100]. §4.1.
- **The tail as counterweight or balance aid**, on r10's trained s42 ends.
  The rail's plant-only effect is 0.2 mm forward and 1.6 mm up, and removing
  it leaves the stance otherwise unchanged (lean, roll, sway, creep and
  margins), with the head about 3.5° lower (COMREV §4; RUNLOG l750) [L139,
  L141]. §4.2.
- **A reward incentive for tail-up.** None was found open loop, under the
  policy's own noise at the two r9 4.19M checkpoints, at r10 s42's onset, or
  at the railed entropy-screen ends [L061, L071, L103, L141]. §4.3.
- **The entropy bonus as a direct driver.**
  - Its gradient reaches log-std only (REVIEW §4.3) [L105].
  - Removing the remaining bonus from two mid-rise r10 states changed
    nothing: s42 6 of 6 against 6 of 6 (ECS `score/score.txt` l53-57)
    [L120]. §4.6.
- **The action dead zone as the cause.** It contributes at most beyond a
  mean of about 1.2 (BRIEF §1 item 3) [L003]. §4.5.
- **Normaliser drift as the source of the loop's growth**, at fixed weights.
  Its direct effect opposes the growth (12/12 in every sweep range), and the
  weights carry more than all of it [L158, L159]. Whether the drift shaped
  training is untested. §4.8.
- **A body position that comes first and pulls the tail up.**
  - The plant gives no mechanical push: the tail map and the cost of tail-up
    are the same in every posture tested [L163].
  - No nose-down or CoM-forward shift came before the tail in any run with
    a tail signal before its rise (the only earlier shifts, r10 s60's raised
    head and its nose-up, CoM-back posture, run the other way), and the same
    shifts came in runs without one [L161, L164]. VPOST did not check the r9
    runs.
  - The posture acts only through the s42 networks' learned pathway, as an
    amplifier from mid-rise on, and s60 parked with the opposite pitch
    sensitivity [L162, L164]. §4.11.
- **Bounding the training-row mean.**
  - P1 (bound 1.0) and BOUND90 (0.90) left the rail in deterministic play
    [L049, L079].
  - BOUND60 (0.60) turned the rail into a park at the bound [L080].
  - All three were screened on r9 only. Whether the results carry over to
    r10, which changed the tail plant, is untested (NEXT 0.2 (e) lines
    109-117, inferred there).
- **Other dead ends.**
  - The policy probe's bank as an early detector [L070]. That was the r9
    read; on r10 s42 the bank's statue_settled row rose ahead of the pitch
    (§8.3) [L164].
  - The habit as the cause of the band arm's K3 falls [L051].

**Open** (§7)

- Why the loop forms in training, and in which seeds and lineages [L064,
  L106, L146]. Its growth sits in the weights downstream of the first layer
  [L158].
- What changed in the policy at about 1.2M, when r10 s42's tail bias first
  rose, before any posture shift, posture pathway or positive loop [L164].
- What drives the outward drift of the training-row mean despite a restoring
  reward slope [L063].
- Whether the drift of the observation normaliser shaped training [L104].
  The no-training normaliser probe of 2026-10-29 [L152] found it an effect of
  the rail that, at fixed weights, lowers the gain [L157, L158]. Whether PPO
  grew the downstream weights partly in answer to it needs a continuation
  with frozen statistics (ECS add-on N), which is training [L159].
- The entropy schedule's role in the first 3.1M steps and in when an onset
  comes. The entropy screen tested neither (ECS `criteria.txt` l246-249)
  [L105, L127].
- Whether r10 changes the rail rate. The first r10 round gave 2 of 3 alarm
  runs against r9's 2 of 5, one-sided Fisher p 0.9286 [L100]. Because
  `ent_coef` reaches 0 at 7,000,000, "no rail by 12M" would not mean
  "removed" if r10 only delayed onsets (REVIEW lines 759-762).
- The railed s42 lineage's 5 mm backward CoM drift. It exists only in the
  tail-up state, and one start checkpoint cannot attribute it [L133, L148].
- Every candidate fix named but never run (§5.2, §7) [L086, L116].

## 3. Everything tried, in date order

This section lists, in date order, every observation, analysis, reward,
plant or training-loss change, probe, screen, round, tool and decision that
bears on the habit, one row each. Lessons are in §6. The evidence for and
against each candidate cause is gathered in §4.

The verdict column uses these words:

- **ruled out**, **narrowed**, **supported** for analyses and probes;
- **adopted** and **not adopted** for changes, tools and decisions;
- **open** where the answer is still unknown;
- **superseded** where a later entry replaced it;
- **record** for a plain measurement that settles nothing by itself.

| When [ledger] | What, and why | Design | Result | Verdict | Full record |

Its zero-action statue settles the tail joints at about 0.01° (r9: -12.07, -11.62, -5.30°, tail_1 on its stop at 30.3 N.m). Segments sit -4.7 to +6.7° to horizontal (r9 -16.2 to -27.5), base-to-tip chord +0.1° (r9 -23.4). The springs carry the tail and the servos hold about 0 N.m. Statue 40/40, 3768.8 ± 16.1 with the tail target re-centred (r9 39/40, 3755.7 ± 19.9); COM 10.7 mm further back. Rejected: springref on tail_1 alone (distal segment still -16.6°), raised ventral stops, stiffness 40 or 160. SR2 leaves tail_3 needing +1.15. ### 3.6 2026-10-28 and 2026-10-29: the entropy screen, the CoM revisit, the clamp test, the normaliser probe and the posture tests

Its sources are the run log's lines 717-757 (lines 717 to 720 in
`stance_2026_10/stance6_results/run_log.txt`, the rest in
`stance_2026_10/ecs_results/run_log.txt`) and the folders ECS, COMREV, CLAMP,
VCLAMP, POL, VPOL, PROBE, VPROBE, PTIME, PPOL and VPOST, whose copies the
change that adds this note keeps (§8.2). The ECS text outputs are also on the
never-merged branch `claude/handoff-ecs` (L128).

## 4. By hypothesis

Each candidate cause below gives its evidence for and against, with ledger
ids pointing to the rows of §3, and its status on 2026-10-29.

### 4.1 The plant forces the pin

The claim: tail_3 cannot reach, within ±1, the pose the policy wants, so the
policy must pin it. Satdiag's version was "The spring is stiffer than the
servo" (SATDIAG §6) [L040].

- **For** [measured]
  - On the r9 statue q3 = -0.0924 + 0.0863a, so a straight distal joint
    needs a = +1.073. At q3 = 0, gravity loads the joint with 13.48 N.m
    against a servo maximum of 12.57 N.m (BRIEF §1 item 5) [L061].
  - tail_1 sat on its ventral stop in every r9 policy; leaving it would need
    a = +1.45 [L018, L026, L061].
  - A level tail on r9 would need commands of +3.50, +2.16 and +1.15 [L084,
    L088].
  - Unclipped, the highest of s42's fixed points lay within about 1 mrad of
    a straight distal tail [L072].
- **Against**
  - The plant does not force the pin. The reward's target pose needs a = 0,
    and tail_2 can lift the tip as high without any channel past 0.9 (BRIEF
    §1 item 5) [measured; L061].
  - Unclipped, the fixed points spread 0.45-1.07, so "The tail aims neither
    for a straight distal joint nor as high as possible" [measured; L072].
  - On r10 a level tail costs a command of about 0, and the rail still
    formed (s42) or the tail parked (s60) [measured; L088, L100].
  - On r10, +1 holds the joint at +5.19°, and the servo carries 7.1 N.m of
    its 50 [measured; L138].
  - The spring was blamed wrongly. At q3 = 0 it adds nothing, and at
    stiffness 40, q3 at a = +1 is -0.0085 against r9's -0.0062 [measured].
    So softening it would not help (BRIEF §1 item 5 line 46, F7) [inferred
    there; L040].
- **Status: ruled out as the cause.** "The plant enables the habit but does
  not cause it" (BRIEF §1 item 5). On r9, "any lasting tail-up preference
  ends on the rail" (same item, under its [measured] heading; a conclusion,
  so [inferred, this record]). r10 removed that
  constraint, and the habit stayed.

### 4.2 The tail as counterweight or balance aid

The claim: the policy raises the tail to move the centre of mass or to
balance.

- **For**
  - r7's narrow-tolerance table called tail_3 an "active counterweight"
    (-8.6° ±8.0°). A constant-hold probe there fell backward onto the tail in
    9 of 10 [measured; L011].
  - Symmetry arm C was "met by actuator saturation", with "the tail tip ...
    pinned as a counterweight; unverified" [L025].
  - The velociraptor parked tail_1 "as a counterweight" (another species)
    [L020].
  - On r9, forcing tail_3 to 0.9 at s42's 1.05M checkpoint moved the
    closed-loop COM -16.8 -> -6.8 mm, and before the s42 flags its COM
    drifted forward (SATDIAG §3) [measured; L038].
  - On r10, clamping the start checkpoint `start_s42` moved the CoM +5.81 mm
    forward with the rail, against without it (20/0) [measured; L148].
- **Against** [measured]
  - On r6, all four tail joints held at +1 stood like the statue: "The tail
    is a bystander" (BOUNCE Addendum 2) [L005].
  - r7's saturated trio was exonerated [L010].
  - Run A's pad edge was not the tail [L024].
  - On r10, tail_3 at its ±12° limits moves the whole-body CoM by only +0.19
    / +0.24 mm fore-aft (a 5.10 kg subtree of a 151.17 kg body) [L130].
  - The rail's plant-only effect is +0.2 mm forward, +1.6 mm up and 0.01°
    of pitch, the same in every policy [L139].
  - Removing the rail from the 12 railed s42 ends leaves lean, roll, sway,
    creep and margins unchanged over 20 s, and return rises +63.6 [L141].
    Imposing it on the 12 s80 ends destabilises them [L142].
  - Railed and unrailed policies do not differ in lean [L132].
  - The rail does not account for the s42-s80 posture gap [L143].
  - Under the judge's joint noise the tail goes down in falls, not up [L051].
- **Status: ruled out on r10's trained s42 ends** ("the rail is not a
  balance or CoM strategy", COMREV §4, inferred there) [L135].
  - [inferred, this record] r9's closed-loop forward shift of about 10 mm is
    far larger than any plant effect measured on r10 (0.2 mm), so it was
    most likely the policy's coupled posture rather than the tail's mass.
    The two were measured on different plants.
  - Still open: the s42 lineage's 5 mm backward CoM drift. It exists only in
    the tail-up state, and the policies' posture coupling to the tail state
    reversed during the continuation [L133, L148]. Arm C's counterweight
    narrative was never verified [L025].

### 4.3 The reward pays for it

- **For**
  - In August, parking was "the cheapest way to be smooth", and the tail
    term priced a tail frozen at a stop as optimal [inferred; L012]. That was
    r7, before #504.
  - stance3's readout: "The policy pays both, so it is buying something
    else" [inferred; L029].
  - At r10 s42's final, holding tail_3 at 0 loses 64.46 (2 of 20 positive).
    But that checkpoint carried a parked command, not a rail [measured;
    L103].
- **Against** [measured]
  - Open loop, the per-step reward falls monotonically as the command rises:
    -0.0971 per step at +1, -77.7 per window, still monotone at sigma 0.16
    [L061].
  - Holding tail_3 at 0 gains wherever a rail was tested:
    - +85.9 and +73.1 per window (r9 stance4 s42, deterministic) [L039];
    - +54.6 and +36.2 under the policy's own noise (r9 4.19M; holding at 0.9
      gives +7.6 and -13.4) [L071];
    - +78.29 at r10 s42's onset [L103];
    - +63.6 at the railed entropy-screen ends [L141].
  - The direct cost is about 80-100 per episode [L140].
- **Status: ruled out at every tested railed checkpoint.**
  - Untested: s42 at its r9 onset (3.1M-3.5M) under full noise, and the
    discounted, normalised objective that PPO actually optimises [L071].
  - "Why training kept the habit is not measured" (SATDIAG §4) [L039].

### 4.4 The tail_stability gyro term

The term: `tail_stability_weight` 0.15 (TOML line 316) prices the tail tip's
angular velocity, reward = -weight x min(\|angvel\| / max, 1)
([environments/shared/base_env.py](../../environments/shared/base_env.py)
lines 585-604; called at
[environments/tyrannosaurus_rex/base_env.py](../../environments/tyrannosaurus_rex/base_env.py)
line 968) [measured, code].

- **For**
  - "the tail term penalises tail-tip angular velocity only, so a tail
    parked motionless at a hard stop is optimal for it" [inferred; L012].
  - TOML lines 267-270: "tail_stability reads only the tail-tip gyro, so a
    tail parked motionless against a hard stop is optimal for it" [measured,
    the config's comment].
- **Against** [measured]
  - The term pays for any still tail, the statue's included, not for tail-up
    as such.
  - The rail changes it by only +0.04 per episode [L140].
  - Since #504 the rail costs about 80-100 per episode through
    `tail_home_pose` and the saturation ramp, and it forms anyway [L140].
- **Status:** [inferred] supported as one reason parking was free in August,
  before #504. Not a driver of the r9 or r10 rail. No run ever varied its
  weight.

### 4.5 The action dead zone (the clipped Gaussian)

The claim: beyond \|mean\| = 1 the clipped command, and so the reward, is
flat, so nothing pulls the mean back.

- **For** [measured]
  - SB3 2.9.0 stores the unclipped sample and its log-prob, and deterministic
    play uses clip(mu), exactly flat beyond +1 [L061].
  - In July the mean was pushed out of the bound [L002].
  - At a mean of 1.11 the clipped-command sensitivity dE[clip a]/dmu is cut
    about 4x (0.22-0.27) [L003].
  - Satdiag's inference [untested]: at mean +1.11 and sigma about 0.14,
    "roughly 79% of training samples clip to +1 and carry no signal to lower
    it" [L039].
- **Against** [measured]
  - The expected reward slope is cut far less: 1.3-1.9x at means 1.05-1.11,
    so the restoring slope keeps about half or more [L003, L061].
  - At stance5 s42's start only 3.7% of training rows had mu > 1 [L062].
  - P1 removed most of the dead zone from training rows (2.5% beyond +1),
    yet the deterministic mean stayed beyond +1 on 21-46% of window steps
    [L049].
  - BOUND90 cut training rows above 0.90 in every pair and still read RAIL
    6/6 [L079, L082].
  - The parked tails (stance3 Run 2's final, r10 s60) sit inside the bound.
- **Status: narrowed.** "The clipped Gaussian's dead zone contributes at most
  beyond about mu 1.2" (BRIEF §1 item 3). Every test of it (P1, BOUND90,
  BOUND60) was on r9; whether the results carry over to r10 is untested (NEXT
  0.2 (e) lines 109-117, inferred there).

### 4.6 The entropy bonus

The claim: the bonus keeps exploration, or a falling bonus lets an extreme
pose settle.

The schedule every r9 and r10 stance run shares: `ent_coef` 0.005, decayed to
0 by 7,000,000 steps with no floor (TOML lines 399-438; REVIEW lines 638-641,
759). It comes from stage 1 (#487: `ent_coef_end` 0.001 -> 0, decay 3M ->
7M; BOUNCE Addendum 5 line 720): the floor was dropped so the bonus would go
away and a still pose, std about 0.05, could be held. Its prediction (std
well under 0.2 at about 4M) was falsified on 2026-10-07 by both r8 runs, and
the anneal was kept; D-D27 changed `log_std_init` instead (TOML lines
444-460) [measured; L156]. [inferred] This decay is the noise decay behind
the -0.967 correlation (L007), and its timing is the part the entropy screen
left untested.

- **For** [measured]
  - On r6, policy std and the number of saturated actuators correlated at
    -0.967 [L007].
  - Every first onset of an alarm run, on both plants, fell while the bonus
    was above 0 [L105].
  - The coefficient was 0.00219 at r10 s42's first tail alarm and 0.00107 at
    s80's first alarm [L105].
- **Against** [measured]
  - The entropy term depends on the state-independent log-std alone; its
    gradient never reaches the mean (STANCE §27.4) [L105].
  - s60's first over-bar episode, and s42's two later spells, came with the
    bonus at 0 [L105].
  - No actuator's std rose over any 1,048,576-step stretch [L105].
  - The entropy screen removed the remaining bonus from two mid-rise r10
    states: s42 railed in 6 of 6 against 6 of 6; s80 flagged (on the neck)
    in 1 of 6 against 1 of 6 [L120]. Arm B's std ended slightly lower, with
    the same outcome [L126].
- **Std before and after onset** [measured]
  - Before onset tail_3's sigma was equal across stance4's three runs
    (0.211, 0.209, 0.217 at 1.05M; SATDIAG §3 line 71) [L038].
  - After onset the railed run ends with the lowest tail_3 std in each
    comparison: stance4 at 2.1M 0.179 against 0.194 and 0.202, "s42 is lowest
    in all 15 dimensions" (SATDIAG §3 line 71); 0.1532 against 0.1762 and
    0.1732 at stance3's 4,194,304 (S3SAT §4); final 0.095 for r10 s42 against
    0.114 (s60) and 0.123 (s80)
    (STANCE §27.3 D-15); r10 s42's tail_3 std is below its other tail
    actuators' from 3,145,728 (0.1694 against 0.1748, 0.1877 and 0.1807;
    READOUTS lines 648-662).
  - [inferred] Lower tail_3 std follows the rail on both plants; whether it
    is cause or effect is unknown.
- **Status: narrowed.** No direct driver, and not needed for a crossing from
  a mid-rise state. Untested:
  - the first 3.1M steps, from scratch (NEXT option 3) [L112];
  - a modest effect, or a delay of the first flag shorter than about 917,504
    steps (ECS `criteria.txt` l250-259) [L127];
  - `log_std_init`, `symmetry_sigma_floor` and `action_saturation_weight`
    (ECS `criteria.txt` l260-262) [L116].

### 4.7 The policy's own feedback loop through the joint-angle observation

The claim: the policy's tail_3 command rises with the tail_3 angle it
observes. Above a certain gain the loop's fixed point lies past +1, so the
command parks at the rail.

- **For** [measured unless said]
  - **Satdiag's swap.** Swapping only the tail_3 joint-angle observation
    moved the raw mean +0.998 -> +0.290 and +0.244 -> +0.943. Zeroing the
    velocity did nothing, and there is no previous-action channel [L037].
  - **The persistence mechanism** [inferred from measured parts]. Loop gain
    about 0.72, amplification about 3.5x, time constant about 75 steps, which
    fits the median 0.99 crossing at step 216. The fixed point lies off the
    training distribution: 3.7% of training rows beyond +1, against 29% of
    deterministic window steps [L062].
  - **Unclipped.** The mean settles at the stable fixed point of a positive
    loop, dmu/da about 0.5-0.6 [L072].
  - **Hold cells.** g about 0.66 predicts CMD 0.95 / 0.60 against a measured
    0.92 / 0.63 [L073].
  - **The clamp sweep.**
    - In 12 of 12 railed s42 ends the gain is positive: median +0.630, range
      +0.480 to +0.910. It was +0.268 at their start checkpoint.
    - The 12 s80 ends have a median of -0.255.
    - Closed-loop holds at 0 / 0.5 / free are linear [L145].
  - **Within an episode** the command climbs from 0 to 0.9 in 2.5 s as the
    tail rises. With the tail held at 0 it stays at 0.35 [L144].
  - The head-up posture follows the same observed angle [L147].
  - **Where the growth sits** [measured, verified; L158, L159]. The
    first-layer column for the tail_3 angle did not change (norm ratio 1.00,
    cosine 0.998). Grafting the start's second hidden layer or output layer
    into each end lowers its gain by 0.298 or 0.240 (12/12); its first layer,
    by 0.046. Per normalised unit the tail_3 sensitivity rose 0.040 -> 0.219
    (x5.4; x4.3-4.65 on matched ranges, VPROBE F4). The normaliser's drift
    worked against the growth (§4.8).
  - **What feeds the offset** [measured, verified with corrections; L162].
    In the s42 lineage, at 3,145,728 and at the ends, the network maps the
    pelvis pitch it sees and its leg angles to the offset c0 (about 0.40 at
    the ends), which the loop multiplies by about 1/(1-G) = 2.7. The posture
    inputs move c0, not G (§4.11).
- **Against, or limits**
  - The estimates differ by method and checkpoint: 0.72, 0.66, 0.5-0.6, and
    0.63 against 0.27 (§8.3).
  - All of them come from deterministic states. The 0.72 is an inference
    from the satdiag swap and the plant gain (BRIEF line 25); 0.66, 0.5-0.6
    and 0.63 / 0.27 are measured.
  - The size of a gain depends on how it is measured [L159, L164]. Under the
    start's statistics the 0-6° sweep reaches tanh compression: the start's
    0.268 is 0.32 over 0-3° and 0.18 over 4.5-6°. s80's sweep is clipped by
    the normaliser beyond 3.1-4.0°, so its gains are about half the unclipped
    slope. A local derivative gives the s80 ends -0.72, against the 0-6°
    fit's -0.28. The signs do not change.
  - **The positive loop is not where the habit started** [measured; L164].
    In r10 s42 the loop was negative before and early in the rise (G -0.250
    at 1,048,576 and -0.386 at 2,097,152, against +0.261 at 3,145,728 on
    VPOST's states); by 2,097,152 the tail bias had been rising since about
    1.20M (L161). In s60
    the loop turned positive (G -0.47 -> +0.32 over 1.05M-8.39M) as its
    offset grew (+0.02 -> +0.61). [inferred, VPOST M2] Offset growth and a
    loop turning positive are common to both r10 tail habits; what feeds the
    offset is not.
  - The fixed-point arithmetic, c0/(1-G), holds the posture fixed. At one s42
    end the posture answers the tail (a = -1 / 0 / +1 gives -2.42 / -1.83 /
    -1.22° of pitch): a negative tail -> posture -> tail path of about -0.1
    per unit that the arithmetic leaves out (VPOST T2-7; one policy, 5
    seeds) [L164].
  - Why training grew the weights is not known [L146, L158]. The
    training-row mean drifts outward despite a restoring slope, and that too
    is unexplained [L063].
- **Status: supported as the persistence mechanism** (COMREV §4, verified).
  "Not shown: why the loop formed in training or what training without it
  would give" (COMREV l129-131) [L146]. Since then the normaliser probe has
  placed the gain's growth in the weights [L158], and VPOST has dated the
  positive loop to after the tail began rising, between 2.1M and 3.15M in r10
  s42 [L164]. Why training grew it is still not known.

### 4.8 Observation-normalisation drift

The claim: VecNormalize's running statistics for the tail_3 angle drift
during training. A shrinking normaliser std scales up the network's input
from that angle and so raises the loop gain of §4.7.

- **For**
  - [measured] D-15: in each r10 run, the observation dimension that drifted
    most at the checkpoints just before onset is the joint that later
    saturates or parks:
    - s42: `qpos.tail_3_pitch`, at 2,097,152, 3,145,728 and 3,407,872 (first
      over-bar episode 3,670,016);
    - s80: `qpos.neck_pitch`, at 3,145,728-3,670,016 (first neck over-bar
      3,801,088);
    - s60: `qpos.tail_3_pitch`, at 6,553,600-7,077,888 (trace DRIVE ONSET
      7,340,032, the first traced checkpoint with a tail pitch CMD >= +0.60:
      REVIEW D-3 line 315, NEXT line 104; the training-row drive of §1.3
      came at 7,962,624, STANCE §27.3 D-2) [L104].
  - [inferred link, untested then] "a shrinking normaliser std on the tail
    angle would raise exactly this gain" (COMREV l126-128) [L104]. The probe
    tested it: the opposite holds (below).
  - [measured, verified] The probe confirms the drift in the ECS
    continuations: the tail_3 angle is the most-drifted of the 63
    dimensions in s42 (median scale x0.437, 0.420-0.451 per run; the next,
    gravity[2], x1.22) [L157].
- **Against**
  - Each joint's command was already rising when its shift came (s60's
    rollout mean +0.44 at 6.3M-7.3M). "Whether the drift is a cause or
    follows the posture is open" (STANCE §27.3 D-15) [L104].
  - That confound is why the entropy screen left add-on N (statistics frozen
    at the start) off [L116].
  - **The normaliser probe** [measured, verified; L157-L159].
    - [inferred, PROBE §6] The drift follows the rail. The frames added
      between checkpoints had tail_3 means of +2.77 to +3.89°, against the
      start's running mean of +0.47°; the start policy already held its tail
      near +3° [L157].
    - A smaller scale shrinks the network's response to a raw angle (gain =
      Jz x scale), so at fixed weights the drift lowers the gain. On the
      ends' states: the start weights with the ends' statistics 0.264 ->
      0.141, the end weights with the start's 0.630 -> 1.143 (12/12 each).
      Swapping the tail_3 angle's statistics alone does the same (1.162)
      [L158].
    - In closed loop the end weights with the start's statistics have a
      gain of 1.086 and stay at the rail (12/12); the start weights with the
      ends' statistics fall to 0.143 and hold the tail lower (+2.13° against
      +3.12°) [L158].
    - Across the 73 s42 checkpoints the gain rose as the scale fell (log-log
      slope -0.81, where a pure scale effect gives +1) [L158].
    - The growth is in the weights downstream of the first layer (§4.7)
      [L158].
    - s80: the tail_3 scale barely moved (x0.98). Its tail statistics are
      inert over the unclipped 0-2.5°, and its tail-only swaps are small but
      not null (the ends with the start's tail statistics: gain -0.005,
      11/12 lower; the start with the ends': return +1.0, 11/1) [L157,
      L159].
- **Corrections to the probe** (VPROBE F9, F10 and its missed items) [L159]
  - The size of the normaliser's share depends on the sweep range: -1.32 /
    -0.90 / -0.47 / -0.26 for 0-3 / 0-6 / 3-6 / 4.5-6°, with an interaction
    term of -0.38. Only its sign (12/12 in every range) is a property of the
    policies.
  - "Without the drift, 8 (closed loop) / 9 (static) of 12 ends would have
    a gain above 1" does not hold. The count is range-dependent (12, 9, 2
    and 1 of 12), and the end weights under the start's statistics run out
    of distribution (z6 about +6.1 in closed loop, against about +2.1 under
    their own).
  - "A shrinking scale can only lower the gain" is true of the variance. The
    drifted mean raises the gain (12/12), by +0.02 or +0.14 depending on the
    order.
  - "Explains none" holds only for the direct, fixed-weight effect. An
    effect through training is not excluded.
  - The probe's basis for a near-linear network (restoring the start's
    variance raises the ends' gain x2.07, against x2.29 for a linear one) is
    weak: under the start's statistics the 0-3° slope is 0.271 and the 3-6°
    slope 0.175 per degree.
- **Instruments** [measured]
  - D-15 reads page `18_obs_normalization` of the training-history report
    (#645; CL lines 263-284; READOUTS line 625) [L095].
  - Since #630, `policy_probe.npz` has also stored, at every PPO update, the
    deterministic action on a fixed bank of ten states under the current and
    the session-start normalisation (CL lines 1155-1164). That is a partial,
    existing read of the probe's question, never used for it. [inferred] Its
    bank is the statue and held ankle leans, not railed stance states, so it
    cannot stand in for the probe [L070].
  - The probe swapped statistics through the harness's own loader
    (`normalizer.obs_rms`). VPROBE checked inside the probe's loop that the
    network received exactly the donor's normalised values on exactly the
    swapped dimensions (maximum difference 0) [L152, L159].
- **Status: ruled out as the source of the gain's growth, at fixed weights;
  open as an influence on training.** The no-training normaliser probe of
  2026-10-29 [L152] asked whether the end policies' loop gain falls back with
  the start's statistics. It does not: it rises, 12 of 12 [L158].
  - [inferred, PROBE §7 as corrected by VPROBE F10] On matched ranges the
    weights' growth (x4.3-4.65 per normalised unit) exceeds the x2.29 that
    compensating the scale alone would need, so compensation cannot be the
    whole story. Part of the growth may still be PPO's answer to the
    compressed input [L159].
  - Only a training counterfactual can settle that: a continuation with
    frozen statistics, the ECS add-on N [L116, L159].

### 4.9 Seed and lineage

- **For a strong seed or lineage effect** [measured]
  - On r6, seed 42 pinned all four tail joints and seed 43 was largely
    unpinned [L008].
  - Seed 42 railed in 2 of 2 r9 recipes and again on r10, its first r10
    alarm 262,144 steps later than in its r9 LTSB run (3,670,016) [L064,
    L106].
  - From s42's r10 3,145,728 state the tail railed in 12 of 12
    continuations; from s80's 4,194,304 state it railed in none (s80 flagged
    on the neck instead) [L120, L123, L124].
  - In the D-D43 CONTROL arm, the rail formed in 3 of 3 from s80's state and
    in 2 of 3 from s42's [L081].
- **Against a fixed outcome per seed** [measured unless said]
  - Seed 60 took the habit in 1 of 3 r9 recipes [L064].
  - Seed 80 railed tail_3 on r9 and saturated its neck on r10 [L106].
  - The actuator is not fixed by the plant either: neck and head parking
    predates r10. On r9 stance3 Run 3 (seed 52) held `neck_pitch` at -0.700
    at 4,194,304 with no flag, and Run 2 (seed 60) held `head_pitch` at -0.52
    beside its tail rail (S3SAT §1) [L155].
  - The r10 criteria's evidence text read the r9 record as not supporting a
    seed effect (p 0.19) (EVID6 lines 384-386) [inferred there].
  - Two no-change draws from one r9 state read frac99 0.92 and 0.17 [L064,
    L053]; GPU training is not bit-reproducible [L106].
  - "Nothing in the data explains which posture a seed ends in" (SATDIAG §6)
    [L041].
  - On r10, s42 and s80 had already differed in the start checkpoints'
    posture [L132].
- **Status: open.**
  - [inferred] From a given mid-rise state the tail outcome looks settled by
    the lineage (12/12 against 0/12, two states).
  - From scratch the seed does not fix it: "a seed fixes the start, not the
    run" (STANCE §27.4).
  - Onset itself is "a trajectory-dependent drift of a low-stakes output"
    (BRIEF §1 item 4) [L064].

### 4.10 Plant revision

- **The plants' tails.**
  - r6 and r7 saturated many joints [L004, L011].
  - On r7/r8, tail_1 rested on its ventral stop under every command, and a
    kp-300 tail_1 servo was measured and left out [L018].
  - On r9 the tail could not leave its stop and so had "no static fore-aft
    authority" [L026]. "r9 cannot hold a level tail" [L084].
  - r10 (SR3) preloads the three tail pitch springs so the statue rests its
    tail level, with the servos at about 0 N.m [L088, L090].
- **For "r10 prevents the rail"** [inferred, before training]. On r9 a level
  tail needed +3.50 / +2.16 / +1.15, and "the +1 pin habit feeds on that".
  On r10 a level tail costs no command, and `tail_home_pose` is re-centred
  on it [L091].
- **Against** [measured]
  - The r10 probe brief itself warned: "A new rail habit could appear ...
    r10 does not guarantee a fix" [L088].
  - Zero-shot, eight r9 checkpoints on SR3 gave no sign of a cure:
    actuator-saturation failures went 27 -> 80, c3 newly pinned tail_2 and
    c5 tail_3, and the tail observations sat 5-8 SD outside the r9
    normaliser's statistics [L089].
  - The first r10 round: s42 RAIL, s60 PARKED, s80 a neck habit [L100].
  - All 12 s42 entropy-screen continuations railed on r10 [L120].
  - The r10 round's evidence text had already noted that r10 does not remove
    the positive loop (EVID6 lines 41-47) [L091].
  - Plant transfer itself causes postures: the r9 checkpoint leans only on
    r10 [L134].
  - Satdiag had warned against a plant fix of this kind (Y1): "It makes the
    tail-up posture cheaper and hides it from the rail rather than
    discouraging it" (SATDIAG §7 line 127, an inference there) [L042]. r10
    s60 then parked tail_3 at +0.642 without saturating [L100]. [inferred,
    this record] That fits the warning.
- **Status: ruled out as a fix.** r10 as shipped, with arm LTSB, does not
  prevent the rail: an existence result (STANCE §27.2) [inferred there].
  Whether r10 changes
  the rate is unknown (2 of 3 against 2 of 5, p 0.9286) [L100].

### 4.11 Body posture drives the tail

The claim, the maintainer's question (logged at 19:55 UTC on 2026-10-29):
the habit may reflect a body positioning that encourages it (RUNLOG l754)
[L160]. Two forms were tested: a mechanical one (in the posture the railed
lineage holds, the plant lifts the tail or makes tail-up cheaper) and a
learned one (the policy raises its tail command when it sees that posture).
A third question was the order: did a posture shift come before the tail
rose?

- **For** [measured, verified with corrections unless said]
  - The railed s42 lineage stands differently: root pitch -1.35° against
    the s80 lineage's -0.25°, CoM fore-aft -25.7 against -32.4 mm. The
    pitch gap was already in the start checkpoints; the fore-aft gap was
    mostly inherited but halved, because the railed lineage moved its CoM
    about 5 mm back during the continuation (11 of 12), and only with the
    tail up (VPOL POL-4; §4.2) [L132, L148].
  - **The learned pathway** (test 2) [L162]. The s42 networks raise the tail
    command when they see the pelvis more nose-down: -0.157 per degree of
    nose-up at the ends (0/12 positive; VPOST -0.163), -0.090 at 3,145,728.
    They read the pitch through the gravity vector (-0.069) and the prey
    direction in pelvis coordinates (-0.087), a second pitch sensor in
    stance, where the prey does not move. The knee and hip angles the lineage
    holds, about 0.5° more negative than s80's, add to it (-0.222 and -0.149
    per degree). 1° of nose-down is worth 1.26° of observed tail angle. The
    CoM is not an input.
  - **The transplant** [L162]. Giving the s42 ends the s80 ends' typical
    orientation, leg and foot inputs (within 1.3 normaliser SD) moves their
    offset c0 0.40 -> about 0.00 (0/12) and their linear rail count 9/12 ->
    0/12, with the gain unchanged (+0.010). Net of the own-lineage control,
    the lineage-specific part is about -0.38 (VPOST T2-2).
  - **The closed-loop pitch illusion** [L162]. Only the observation is
    changed; the plant is untouched. Shown a pelvis 1.1° more nose-up, the
    s42 ends lower their own tail command (-0.190; 0/11 and one tie) and
    their railed episodes fall 0.671 -> 0.154; over 20 s on 5 seeds, 37/60 ->
    8/60. Shown it 1.1° more nose-down, they rail more (0.671 -> 0.896).
    VPOST reproduced the ±1.1° arms on its own seeds (railed 28/40 -> 0/40 on
    4 ends). The gravity and accelerometer channels alone do about a third
    (0.671 -> 0.392); VPOST did not check that arm or the 20 s check.
  - Across the 12 s42 ends, the more nose-down a policy stands, the larger
    its offset (rho -0.61; VPOST -0.64) [L162; correlational, n = 12].
- **Against** [measured, verified with corrections unless said]
  - **No mechanical push** (test 3) [L163]. In the postures the s42 ends
    hold, the tail_3 angle reached is +0.026 + 5.160a°, against the statue's
    +0.026 + 5.159a and the s80 ends' +0.024 + 5.152a; a = 0 holds the tail
    at the target in every posture. Tail-up costs 0.0989 reward per step in
    the s42 posture and 0.0984 in the s80 posture (VPOST: 0.0982 against
    0.0978, about 0.0004 dearer, not cheaper). A pure 1° nose-down rotation
    lifts the railed tail by 0.013°, 1/60 or less of what the policy's own
    pitch pathway adds per degree. VPOST did not re-check the static
    torques, the term split or the pure rotation (T3-3).
  - **No nose-down or CoM-forward shift came first** (test 1) [L161, L164].
    The only shifts that came before a rise were r10 s60's (its head raised
    about 3M steps earlier, then nose-up and CoM back), in the opposite
    direction. Before its rise, r10 s42 stood 0.24-0.33° more nose-up than
    s80, with its CoM 1.6-1.8 mm further back; the lineage's nose-down offset
    opened only at 2.1-3.15M, as the tail rose. VPOST's change points put the
    tail first: the policy's tail_3 command mean (mu) at 1.204M, the pitch
    about 0.17M later (bootstrap 95% 0.07-0.27M),
    the CoM forward about 0.4M after the tail (F1, F11). At a fixed
    observation the policy's tail_3 command was already rising at 1.3-1.6M
    (M3; read through each checkpoint's own normaliser).
  - **The pathway and the positive loop came after the rise began** (VPOST
    M1) [L164]. At 1,048,576 the s42 network had no nose-down-to-tail
    pathway (+0.005 per degree) and a negative loop (G -0.250). At
    2,097,152, with the tail already rising (c0 +0.238), the pathway was
    -0.020, about 1/8 of its 3,145,728 value, G was -0.386, and the s80
    transplant removed only 0.058 of the offset.
  - **Not general** (VPOST M2) [L164]. r10 s60, which also parked, has the
    opposite pitch sensitivity: seeing more nose-up raises its tail command
    (+0.085 per degree at 5.24M, +0.027 at 7.34M), while its loop turned
    positive and its offset grew. Its main tail rise came with nose-up, the
    CoM back and the head raised (direction confirmed by VPOST F4, at about
    2/3 of test 1's magnitudes) [L161].
  - **Not sufficient** (test 1) [L161]. The same shifts came without a tail
    rise: r10 s60 at 5.0-5.5M (nose-down 0.3°, CoM 7 mm forward, tail flat),
    r10 s80 (head raised strongly), the ECS s80 lineage (CoM +4.0 mm, 9 of
    12; VPOST F7: +3.6 mm, 9/3) and r9 s60 (about 1.0° nose-down; VPOST did
    not check r9).
  - The s80 lineage has no pathway (-0.019 per degree, 5/7) and a negative
    loop. Shown a nose-down pelvis in closed loop, its command rises by
    +0.025 and no rail forms (0 of 240) [L162].
  - Within the s42 continuations, posture and tail part once the rail
    forms: over the whole window the pitch returns (+0.08°, 8/4) and the CoM
    moves back (-5.1 mm, 1/11) while the tail keeps rising (VPOST F6)
    [L161].
  - On r9, holding the tail mean at 0.6 in training (b60) left s42 more
    nose-down than its controls (-1.88° against -1.39°). VPOST did not check
    this (F8) [L161].
- **Status: narrowed** [inferred, from the verified results above].
  - Ruled out: a mechanical push from the posture, and a posture shift that
    comes first and pulls the tail up.
  - Supported within its scope: in the s42 lineage, from 3,145,728
    (mid-rise) on, the networks map the nose-down posture they see and
    their leg angles to the tail offset that the learned loop amplifies to
    the bound. The posture is an amplifier of a habit already under way,
    learned with it, not its origin. VPOST corrected the tests' "the habit
    tracks the s42 body positioning" to this (T2-7).
  - Specific to s42: s60 parked with the opposite pitch sensitivity.
  - The earliest event found is a posture-independent rise of the tail bias
    at about 1.2M in r10 s42 (§7). Whether training built the pathway because
    of the posture, or the two grew together, cannot be tested without
    training (PPOL §0) [L165].
  - Unchecked by VPOST: every r9 claim of test 1 (F8 among them); the
    reading that about three quarters of the start's nose-down pitch
    persists with the tail held at 0 (F10; at one end, `s42_A_1111`, a lower
    tail gives more nose-down, -1.83° at a = 0 against -1.22° at +1); test
    2's gravity-only arm and 20 s check; test 3's static plant and pure
    rotation (T3-3) [L161-L164].

### 5.2 Diagnostic holds and forcings (no training)

These changed a command inside a rollout, never a policy.

- **Hold tail_3 at 0.**
  - r9 satdiag: +85.9 / +73.1, no falls [L039].
  - r9 noise value: +54.6 / +36.2 [L071].
  - r10 D-9: +78.29 at onset, -64.46 at the final [L103].
  - Clamp (i): +63.6, stance otherwise unchanged, head 3.5° lower [L141].
  - Where it was measured, the policy's own tail_3 output fell: to +0.244 /
    +0.188 (satdiag) [L037]; to +0.316 / +0.321 at s42 and +0.191 / +0.205 at
    s80 (noise-value probe, stochastic / deterministic; SCREEN
    `probes/noisevalue/analyze.out` lines 7, 19, 35, 47) [L071]; and to 0.33
    (clamp (i)) [L141]. D-9 recorded no own-output value (REVIEW lines
    456-466).
- **Force tail_3 to 0.9 or +1.**
  - r9: at 1.05M, forcing 0.9 moved the COM forward 10 mm with no flag
    [L038].
  - Clamp (ii): -179.9 with destabilised s80 stances [L142].
- **Swap the joint-angle observation**: the command follows the observed
  angle [L037, L145].
- **Unclip the control range (±0.40)**: the mean settles at fixed points
  0.45-1.07 [L072].
- **Clamp the start checkpoints**: the policies' coupling to the tail state
  reversed during the continuation [L148].
- **Swap the normaliser's statistics** (the probe): the end weights with the
  start's statistics raise their closed-loop gain 0.632 -> 1.086 and stay at
  the rail (12/12); the start weights with the ends' statistics lower it
  0.262 -> 0.143 and hold the tail lower (+2.13° against +3.12°) [L158,
  L159].
- **Graft actor layers between start and end**: the start's second hidden
  layer or output layer lowers an end's gain by 0.298 or 0.240, its first
  layer by 0.046 (12/12) [L158].
- **Show the policy a different pelvis pitch** (posture test 2, observation
  only): seen 1.1° more nose-up, the s42 ends' railed episodes fall 0.671 ->
  0.154 (37/60 -> 8/60 at 15-20 s, a 20 s check VPOST did not re-run); seen
  1.1° more nose-down, they rise to 0.896. Seen 1.1° more nose-down, the s80
  ends do not rail (0 of 240) [L162].
- **Transplant the other lineage's posture inputs** (observation only): the
  s80 ends' orientation, legs and feet remove the s42 ends' offset (c0 0.40
  -> about 0.00) without changing the gain [L162].
- **Replace the tail command in the policies' own held postures** (posture
  test 3, the plant alone): the tail map and the cost of tail-up are the
  same in the s42, s80 and statue postures [L163].
