## 6. Lessons learned

### 6.1 Technical: about the habit, and about RL on position servos

1. **A saturated command is not a saturated joint or motor.** Read commands
   per actuator; force columns cannot see this habit.
   - Satdiag found the joint 0.216 rad below its stop at 0.26 of its force
     limit, while the ledger's force columns read 0.272-0.283 and its
     force-limited fraction 0 (SATDIAG §1) [L035, L043].
   - The stance reward pilots had already noted that the gate key reads raw
     normalised commands, not force (PILOTS §2 lines 209-210) [L023].
   - On r10, +1 holds the joint at only +5.2°, about 43% of its 12° upward
     range [inferred, this record; L138].
2. **\|action\| = 1 is not one physical event.** A saturated tail joint moves
   8-12°, a toe 37.5°. Order saturation by degrees (BOUNCE Addendum 3) [L006].
3. **Authority the reward cannot see will be spent.**
   - A constant command is free under smoothness and jerk.
   - A tail-tip gyro term prices any still tail as perfect.
   - So parking at a stop was "the cheapest way to be smooth" (NARROW §1
     item 3, lines 50-55) [inferred; L012].
   - Price on the policy's own command: on the filtered command saturation
     had been charged 222x too little (D-D27) [L022].
4. **Pricing a bound moves where the policy parks; it does not remove the
   posture.**
   - The 0.9 ramp produced a head parked at -0.890 and a tail at +0.841, just
     under it [L014, L028].
   - BOUND60 produced a tail parked at 0.600-0.632 [L080].
   - Satdiag predicted the same for a lower ramp start [L042].
   - So D-D43 counts "rail cleared but tail parked" as NOT ADOPTED [L069].
5. **A training-loss bound acts on training rows, not on the deterministic
   posture the gate reads.**
   - P1: training rows beyond +1 fell to 2.5%, while the deterministic mean
     stayed beyond +1 on 21-46% of window steps.
   - BOUND90 cut the rows above 0.90 in every pair and kept the rail (TAILEXP
     check 1; STANCE §24.6) [L050, L052].
   - "P1 works as a wall, not a pull" (TAILEXP Findings).
6. **A habit can cost reward and persist.**
   - Every railed checkpoint tested gains from holding tail_3 at 0
     [L039, L071, L103, L141].
   - Persistence runs through a deterministic fixed point that training
     rarely samples [L062], via a learned positive-feedback loop through the
     observed joint angle, with a gain below 1 and a fixed point past the
     bound [L145].
   - [inferred] A reward penalty that the deterministic policy never meets
     in training cannot pull it back.
7. **Harmless to balance is not harmless.**
   - In August the saturated tail was a mechanical bystander (`only_tail`
     stood at 3254.0).
   - In October the rail failed the gate (K1 on stance3 Run 2, stance4 s42,
     stance5 s42, stance5r s80; r10 s42's certificate) without falls, and it
     cost reward (BOUNCE Addendum 2; SATDIAG §4; STANCE §27.3 D-9) [L009].
   - "Conspicuous, not causal" was a verdict on the r6 bounce. On the stance
     gate the rail is itself the failure.
8. **Habits move between actuators**, so fixing one channel can relocate the
   rail.
   - On r6, seed 43 had tail_1_yaw at the opposite limit [L008].
   - On r9, stance5r s70 held tail_2 at +0.767 with no flag, and stance3 Run 3
     held `neck_pitch` at -0.700 [L154, L155].
   - On SR3 zero-shot, c3 (that s70 final) pinned tail_2 at +1 [L089].
   - On r10, seed 80 moved from tail_3 to `neck_pitch_act` [L106, L111].
9. **A plant fix is untested until a policy trains on it.**
   - Zero-shot policies cannot predict it: the tail observations sat 5-8 SD
     outside the r9 normaliser's statistics [L089, L093].
   - "Hold physics changes as drafts until a training screen" (STANCE §12)
     held again.
   - A plant change under a trained policy also creates postures of its own:
     the r9 checkpoint leans only on r10 [L134].
10. **Watch at 0.60, not 0.9.**
    - A 0.9 watch misses parked tails (+0.888 at s42's final, +0.896 at Run
      2's 11.53M).
    - The 0.60 line separates every r9 saturated checkpoint (+0.87 to +1.0)
      from the unaffected runs (never above +0.34) [L044]. Both are tail_3
      figures; stance5r s70's tail_2 sat at +0.767 [L154]. A run within about
      0.05 of the line classifies by chance [L044].
11. **To know whether a posture is needed, take it away.**
    - The s42 ends kept their stance without the rail: "tolerated, not
      needed" (COMREV §4) [L141].
    - The s80 ends were destabilised when it was imposed [L142].
    - The CoM tests cannot see the habit on the s42 policies; "The
      actuator-saturation ledger stays the right detector" [L149].
12. **From s42's mid-rise r10 state (3,145,728) the rail formed whatever
    the remaining entropy bonus**: 12 of 12 continuations railed with or
    without it (ECS) [L127]. [inferred] A large effect of the remaining bonus
    was not seen; a modest effect or a delay was not tested (ECS
    `score/score.txt` l55), and from s80's mid-rise state the tail did not
    rail at all [L123].
13. **Saturation appears as noise decays.**
    - On r6 std and the number of saturated actuators correlated at -0.967
      [L007].
    - Under the judge's joint noise the pinned state goes away (>= +0.9 on at
      most 0.012 of steps) [L051, L062].
    - [inferred] Deterministic readouts are where this habit lives, so judge
      and probe them deterministically, and under noise too.
14. **A drifting normaliser is a record before it is a cause; separate it
    from the weights before blaming it.**
    - The tail_3 angle's statistics drifted most because the tail rose: the
      frames added sat at +2.77 to +3.89°, against a running mean of +0.47°
      [L157].
    - At fixed weights the drift lowered the loop gain (12/12 in every sweep
      range); the weights downstream of the first layer carried more than all
      of the growth [L158, L159].
    - [inferred] A statistics swap and a layer graft answer the fixed-weight
      question without training. Whether a drift shaped training is a
      separate question, for a frozen-statistics continuation [L159, L166].
15. **A posture can feed a habit through what the policy sees, not through
    the body; and what amplifies a habit late need not be where it began.**
    - The plant's tail map and the cost of tail-up are the same in every
      posture [L163]. The s42 networks read the nose-down pitch (through
      gravity and the prey direction in pelvis coordinates) and their leg
      angles as a tail offset, which the learned loop amplifies [L162].
    - That pathway and the positive loop grew after the tail began rising.
      The first event found was a posture-independent tail bias at about
      1.2M, and s60 parked with the opposite pitch sensitivity [L161, L164].
    - [inferred] Test the plant and the network separately (a plant check in
      the held postures; an observation-only illusion with the plant
      untouched), at checkpoints before the rise and in more than one
      lineage [L166].

### 6.2 Process: screens, pre-registration, outcome-blindness, verification, time

1. **Judge several checkpoints and read the whole ledger.**
   - The rail forms, clears and re-forms: stance3 Run 2 ran in four spells to
     11.14M with a clean final. A cleared rail can be a swing to the opposite
     park, not a return to neutral: r10 s42's tail_3 command went to -0.56 at
     6.95M between its spells, and stance3 Run 2's fell to +0.191 (tail down)
     at 8.39M [L028, L099, L101].
   - The notebook's own gate judged early robust_best pairs (0.9M-2.0M on
     r10; 917,504 and 393,216 on r9) and "could not see a late rail".
   - A gate read only at the final hides rails both ways: r10 s80 failed at
     6,291,456 and passed at the final, and s42 the reverse (STANCE §23.4
     lines 3430-3433 and RUNLOG l327-330 for the r9 pairs; STANCE §19.5 item
     3, §27.3 D-8; REVIEW lines 437-448, 743-748) [L031, L108].
   - "Judge several checkpoints" was already a lesson of the foot work
     (STANCE §12).
2. **Watch the training ledger with an alarm, and make it name the
   actuator.**
   - stance4 s42's 70 and stance3 Run 2's 44 flagged evaluations (at least 5
     of 10 episodes; Run 2 had 39 with 8 or more) went unwatched until the
     alarm [L030].
   - Naming the actuator took checkpoint downloads and offline rollouts for
     every run until #640 [L043, L058].
3. **Do not name what has not been measured.** The stance4 report called
   s42's actuator "tail" before anything measured it. Its verification caught
   the claim (RUNLOG l177-180) [L033].
4. **One draw per arm cannot size an effect.** Two no-change continuations
   from one state read 0.925 and 0.171 (TAILEXP check 2) [L053]. Use
   replicates per arm per state (K = 3 in D-D43, 6 in the ECS) and a matched
   control. "A seed fixes the start, not the run" (STANCE §27.4).
5. **Small from-scratch rounds cannot show a lower rate.**
   - 0 of n against 2 of 5 gives p 0.36 (n 3), 0.22 (5), 0.13 (8) and 0.095
     (10); "a 0 of n result needs n >= 16 to reach 0.05" (BRIEF §4; CRIT6
     line 294) [L077].
   - Screen as continuations with replicates first, and read a three-run
     round as existence, not rate [L100].
6. **Have skeptics read a design before any compute.** Two skeptics stopped a
   five-seed round with an unsupported rationale and an endpoint near a
   manipulation check; all 17 of their findings held (BRIEF Appendix A)
   [L065]. Their D4 showed that a strict "no worse than the recorded band on
   K2, K3 and K4" harm rule would pass a bound with no cost at all only
   0.15-0.68 of the time, so set harm rules with a stated false-veto rate
   (BRIEF §4 lines 240-243, Appendix A D4 line 287) [L065].
7. **Pre-register, keep fails as fails, stay outcome-blind.**
   - D-D40 5a stopped no run on an alarm [L034].
   - The ECS wrote its predicted outcome before running [L113].
   - It opened no attempt log before all 24 continuations were decided, and
     two lenses re-derived its reading with their own code [L119, L121].
   - The r10 round was read once, from one settled fetch [L100].
8. **Verify adversarially, and anchor comparisons on bit-identity.**
   - Verifiers changed five readings of the clamp and the policies lens: the
     servo-spring split, five head-clearance losers not two, where the 5 mm
     drift lives, the loop gain measured rather than inferred, and the r9
     lean explanation refuted [L150].
   - The clamp reproduced D-9 to 9.095e-12 before it ran anything new
     [L137].
   - The verifiers of the normaliser probe and of the posture tests changed
     readings again: the probe's "how much" (range-dependent, and out of
     distribution in its swaps), and the posture pathway's origin and
     generality (it grew after the rise began, and s60 has the opposite
     sensitivity). Each first re-ran the work with its own code (for the
     probe, bit-for-bit, 460 of 460 episodes) [L159, L164].
9. **Keep the pre-registration lean.** The r10 round's criteria grew through
   twelve versions (v12 1,019 lines, against stance3/4/5's 79, 142 and 190)
   before the maintainer chose LEAN-R (STANCE §26.5) [L097].
10. **Run a screen's gate on the untouched base before pinning it.** The D-D43
    gate failed on an invariant the band commit breaks by design, and had to
    be re-pinned (RUNLOG l549-553) [L075].
11. **Keep a durable copy of scratch code.**
    - P1's code (`scratch_tailfix`, branch `scratch/tail-mean-bound` at
      `1d2d22b4`) was by decision left out of the handoff branch, which
      carried small text files only: "the never-push tail-fix code is
      described, not pushed" (RUNLOG l448, l500;
      `scratchpad/handoff/HANDOFF.md` lines 106-110, session scratch, not
      kept; the never-merged branch `claude/handoff-stance5r` carries it). The
      next session did not have it, and the bound was
      re-created from the report (BRIEF lines 95-96) [L076].
    - Durable orphan branches came after: `claude/handoff-tailscreen`,
      `claude/handoff-stance6`, `claude/handoff-ecs` [L069, L128].
12. **Know what a mid-run fetch holds.** A ledger newer than the placed
    `progress.json` read as "STALE" without being a resume (RUNLOG l461-467)
    [L055]. The r10 round fetched once, after the runs ended.
13. **Name what a statistic counts.** The r10 round's statistic A counted
    alarms on any actuator, so a neck alarm stood against r9's tail alarms
    (REVIEW lines 110-112) [L109]. D10 was first decided as tail-alarm runs
    (19:01 UTC) and re-worded at 22:18 UTC to any-actuator alarm runs, to
    match how LTSB's r9 2 of 5 had been counted (RUNLOG l621, l627-628)
    [L096].
14. **Read long enough.** A 5-10 s window under-reads slow lateral settling;
    a held lean or creep needs at least 20 s (VPOL `NOTES.txt` l185-187)
    [L150].
15. **What saved time** [inferred, from the records].
    - Report-only telemetry replaced checkpoint downloads (#640, #638) [L058,
      L060].
    - CPU continuations and no-training probes answered questions without
      from-scratch rounds: P1, the D-D43 screen, the noise-value and
      unclipped probes, D-9, the clamp, the normaliser probe and the
      posture tests [L049, L071, L072, L078, L141, L158, L162].
16. **What cost time** [measured events].
    - Naming the actuator by rollouts each time [L043].
    - Re-creating lost scratch code [L076].
    - Twelve criteria versions [L097].
    - A container restart during the ECS build's fixer (RUNLOG l727) [L117].
    - A container restart at about 20:50 UTC on 2026-10-29, which stopped
      the probe's verifier and posture tests 2-3 mid-run; both resumed from
      their caches (RUNLOG l756) [L159, L160].
    - A failed gate pin [L075].
    - The hand transcription of `evaluations.npz` was cut off and replaced by
      an exact-copy script (STANCE §27.1) [L110].
17. **Correct the record when a measurement moves it**, and keep both texts
    visible (§8.3). Examples: tail_2 was not on its stop on r9; the spring
    was not the limit; the count of affected runs grew from 2 of 5 to 4 of
    10; stance4 s42's actuator went from "not recorded" to tail_3.
18. **Probe before the rise, not only from the mid-rise start.** Neither
    posture report probed a checkpoint before the ECS start (3,145,728),
    which tests 2-3 took as "the start"; there the deterministic tail command
    was already 0.60. The r10 F2 checkpoints at
    1,048,576 and 2,097,152 were local, and probing them changed the reading:
    no pathway and a negative loop before the rise (VPOST M1, F12) [L164,
    L167].
19. **Give a timing or a share with the method that produced it.**
    - "Started together, within one bin" was an artefact of fixed thresholds
      (0.1 command against 0.3°). A noise-matched change point with a block
      bootstrap put the tail about 0.17M ahead (VPOST F1) [L161, L164].
    - The probe's normaliser share ran from -1.32 to -0.26 with the sweep
      range, and its "8 of 12 above 1 without the drift" evaluated co-adapted
      weights out of distribution; only the signs held 12/12 in every range
      (VPROBE) [L159].
    - At the same ends, the closed-loop railed share moved with the seed
      sample (0.82 against 0.67), and s80's loop gain with its definition
      (-0.72 local, -0.28 by the 0-6° fit) (VPOST M6) [L164].
    - [inferred] Read the sign that holds across methods, and give each size
      with its method [L167].

## 7. Open questions and the candidate next steps already proposed

Who decides: the maintainer. Nothing trains, and no gate, reward, plant or
criterion changes, without the maintainer's approval. Any training change
needs criteria approved before the first training step, as for D-D43, D-D51
and the entropy screen.

**Done since the first draft.** The tail normaliser probe (chosen at 17:28
UTC on 2026-10-29, no training) [L151, L152] and the three posture tests
(logged at 19:55 UTC) [L160] are done and verified. COMREV §6 item 4
framed the probe's question: "re-run the end policies with the start's
normaliser to see whether the loop gain falls back. If it does,
normalisation drift is the lever to study, not entropy." It does not fall
back. It rises (closed loop 0.632 -> 1.086, 12/12), and the weights carry the
growth; whether the drift shaped training is untested [L158, L159] (§4.8).
The posture tests found a learned posture-to-tail pathway in the s42 lineage
from mid-rise on, no mechanical push, and no nose-down or CoM-forward shift
before the tail [L161-L164] (§4.11).

**Open questions**

1. Why does the positive loop form in training, and why does its gain grow
   (0.27 -> 0.63 over 1,572,864 steps) [L145, L146]?
   - The earliest event found is now a posture-independent tail bias. In r10
     s42 the policy's tail_3 command mean began rising at about 1.20M, about
     0.17M before the nose-down pitch, and at a fixed observation the tail
     command was rising by 1.3-1.6M. Around then the network had little or
     no posture-to-tail pathway (+0.005 per degree at 1,048,576; -0.020 at
     2,097,152, about 1/8 of its 3,145,728 value) and a negative loop (G
     -0.250 at 1,048,576, -0.386 at 2,097,152); both grew between 2.1M and
     3.15M [L161, L164]. What changed in the policy at about 1.2M is not
     known. The fixed-observation reading goes through each checkpoint's own
     normaliser, so it does not separate the drift [L164].
   - The weights, not the normaliser, carry the gain's growth: it sits in the
     second hidden layer and the output layer, not in the first layer's
     tail_3 column [L158, L159]. Which reward gradient grew them is not
     known.
2. Why in some lineages and seeds, and not others [L064, L106, L120]?
3. What drives the outward drift of the training-row mean while the open-loop
   reward falls (-0.122 ± 0.037 per mini-batch on tail_3's bias) [L063]?
4. Did the normaliser's drift shape training [L104]? At fixed weights it is
   an effect of the rail and lowers the gain (12/12 in every sweep range)
   [L157, L158]. Whether PPO grew the downstream weights partly in answer to
   the compressed input (x4.3-4.65 per normalised unit, against the x2.29
   that full compensation needs) needs a training counterfactual: a
   continuation with frozen statistics (ECS add-on N) [L116, L159].
5. Does the entropy schedule matter in the first 3.1M steps, or for when an
   onset comes [L105, L127]?
6. Does r10 change the rail rate [L100]?
7. Is the railed s42 lineage's 5 mm backward CoM drift the rail's doing? It
   exists only in the tail-up state, with one start checkpoint [L133, L148].
8. What is tail-up worth at r9 s42's onset (3.1M-3.5M) under full noise, and
   under the discounted, normalised objective [L071]?
9. Did r9 s42's 1.3-1.7M forward COM drift track the tail command? Answering
   it needs `gate_progress.npz` or the 1,572,864 checkpoint, and neither was
   fetched (SATDIAG §3, §8) [L038].
10. Why does the hold-0 value change sign at r10 s42's final (-64.46) when it
    is positive everywhere else (§8.3) [L103]?
11. Which actuators were never named?
    - Arm C (narrative only) [L025].
    - The 2026-09 nodes (8, 1 and 40 episodes) [L019].
    - The saturated ledger episodes of every r9 run without an alarm but
      stance4 s60, first at 655,360 (stance3 Run 3, flagged once at
      4,718,592), 7,864,320 (stance4 s52), 6,946,816 (stance5 s52),
      11,403,264 (stance5 s60) and 8,126,464 (stance5r s70) (EVID6 Table A,
      lines 392-401); LTSB's late window held 3 in 840 (s52 2, s60 1), and
      s52's fresh-seed final failed only the saturation bar (EVID6 lines
      122-129) [L041, L154].
    - stance3 Run 1 was never checked for an alarm [L064].
12. What do the ECS's per-actuator training-row shares for tail_3 show? They
    are stored in ECS `score/read/<name>/read.json` and were never summarised
    [L121].
13. Clamp variants never run: tail_1 or tail_2 clamps, stochastic clamps,
    other reset-noise levels, and more than one start checkpoint per lineage
    (COMREV l129-131) [L149].
14. What feeds r10 s60's tail offset? Its pitch sensitivity is the opposite
    of s42's (seeing more nose-up raises its command), while its loop also
    turned positive [L164].
15. Did training build the s42 posture pathway because of the posture, or
    did the two grow together? Not testable without training [L162, L165].
16. Which posture claims stand unverified? VPOST did not check any r9 claim
    of test 1 (F8 among them), the start-checkpoint clamp reading (F10),
    test 2's gravity-only arm and 20 s check, or test 3's static plant and
    pure rotation (T3-3) [L161-L164].
17. Posture variants never run: the r9 lineage's posture pathway; tail_1 and
    tail_2 pathways and leg-angle illusions in closed loop; own-noise play;
    and the r10 F2 `diagnostics.npz` (training-time head height), which is
    on Drive and was not fetched (PPOL `NOTES.txt` l228-229; PTIME
    `NOTES.txt` l339-342) [L161, L162].

**Candidate next steps already proposed** (none chosen beyond the probe and
the posture tests)

- **From the r10 review** (NEXT, ranked) [L112]:
  - (2) a no-training addendum: the review's named gaps and KI's r10 checks
    on the finals;
  - (3) a from-scratch two-arm entropy round, the only test of the early
    phase, about 170-230 L4-hours. Its power at one-sided alpha 0.05: with a
    complete cure (true 2/3 against 0), 0.68 at 6 runs per arm and 0.91 at
    8; with the rate halved (2/3 against 1/3), 0.18 at either size; at r9's
    rate against a cure (0.4 against 0), 0.18 and 0.41 (REVIEW lines
    670-673, computed there);
  - (4) an r10 stance parent and a new walking pilot;
  - (5) two more unchanged LTSB runs on r10.
- **The ECS add-ons C, N and S**, each needing its own approval [L116]. N
  (VecNormalize frozen at the start) is the training-side form of the
  probe's question. The probe answered the fixed-weight side (the drift
  follows the rail and lowers the gain), so N is now the one test of whether
  the drift shaped training [L157-L159].
- **D-D43's routing for BOUND90**: the deterministic-state bound variant, a
  stronger dose (about 5.2), or the posture decision [L078, L086].
- **The posture skeptics' additions**: a BOUND60 persistence screen to the
  K3 slots, and a lower bound b of about 0.45-0.50 [L085, L086].
- **Option B**, tail_3 reach: held [L067].
- **Satdiag's cheapest checks** (SATDIAG §8 lines 130-134):
  - fetch `gate_progress.npz` and `diagnostics.npz` for stance4 s42, s52 and
    s60;
  - fetch and roll the 1,572,864 checkpoint;
  - run stochastic rollouts of s42 at 2.1M with and without tail_3 held.
    The noise-value probe ran this third check at 4.19M, not 2.1M [L071].
- **CoM-P1** as a report-only reading in the next round's pre-registration
  (chosen at 17:28 UTC on 2026-10-29). It is not specific to the tail [L151].

## 9. Update 2026-10-30: the early-bias probe, the early-warning tests and the two critic-blind screens

*Appended 2026-10-30. §1–§8 are as the change that added this note left
them, but for the date line and the status block at the top; their figures
stand as written there, and §9.3 gives the one correction this update makes
to them.* This section adds the work of 2026-10-29 23:47 UTC to 2026-10-30
16:32 UTC, decisions D-D56 to D-D58
([../BEHAVIOR_RECIPES_PLAN.md](../BEHAVIOR_RECIPES_PLAN.md) §6.2): a
no-training probe of what first pushes the tail command up, an early-warning
curve with two follow-ups, and two scratch CPU training screens that hid the
`tail_3` angle from the critic, from s42's r10 checkpoints at 1,048,576 and
2,097,152 steps. Its sources are the run log's lines 760 to 801, copied as
[stance_2026_10/critic_blind_results/run_log.txt](stance_2026_10/critic_blind_results/run_log.txt)
(copy line = N - 759; "RUNLOG lN" as in the rest of this record), the
overnight report beside it, and the folders named below, which the records
change of D-D58 (3) keeps. Each row of §9.1 is a ledger row, L168 to L186,
and L187 holds §9.8's lessons. Labels are as in the rest of this record.
This record itself was merged into `main` as #654 at 13:36:32 UTC on
2026-10-30 (RUNLOG l790). Nothing here is certified, and nothing trains on
`main`.

### 9.2 What first pushes the tail command up (the early-bias probe, verified with corrections)

[measured, VEB `vb_aggregate.txt` l27-150 and RUNLOG l771, unless said; L170, L171]

- **Only the policy-gradient term moves the tail command's mean.** The
  entropy and value terms change it by exactly 0; the mirror term is small
  and changes sign (MR §1).
- **Just after s42's onset there is already a push, and it is s42's own.**
  At 1,310,720 steps, about 0.1M after s42's tail began to rise (1.204M),
  s42 has an upward policy-gradient pull on `tail_3` carried by `tail_3`'s
  own exploration noise: +0.00115 ± 0.00032 per epoch (t 3.6), with the
  r9-style bias pull at -0.040 and the critic's slope on the tail angle
  positive. s80 has none then (+0.00011 ± 0.00025, t 0.4) and gets the same
  kind of push about 0.8M later. The first probe (EB), on checkpoints 1.05M
  apart, missed it; the verifier fetched the 1,310,720 checkpoints read-only
  from Drive.
- **From 2.10M the critic is the source.** On the policy's own states the
  GAE pull exceeds the critic-free Monte Carlo pull (GAE minus MC +0.00372 ±
  0.00099, t 3.7, at 2.10M; +0.02386 ± 0.00301, t 7.9, at 3.15M), while the
  collected rewards push down: the critic's bootstrapped value pays for a
  higher tail. The push grows tenfold in s42 by 3.15M and is gone in s80
  (EB).
- **Not settled.** At the onset the source is unresolved (GAE minus MC
  +0.00048 ± 0.00045 at 1.31M), and it is not seen on the statue_settled
  state. Why the critic comes to value a raised tail is not known; the
  reading that the tail angle stands in for "how far into the episode" is a
  hypothesis the verifier did not settle, and the probe's own critic-error
  slope was omitted (MR §1; RUNLOG l771).

### 9.3 Can the habit be seen coming? (the early-warning curve and its follow-ups, verified)

[measured; L173-L176]

- **The loop flips just before the rail, but s80 drifts there too.** The
  tail loop's gain G (0-1° sweep) is strongly negative early (-0.73 s42,
  -1.38 s60, -1.38 s80 at 1,048,576) and turns positive one saved checkpoint
  before the first tail over-bar episode: s42 +0.35 at 3,145,728 (its first
  over-bar episode at 3,670,016), s60 +0.31 at 7,340,032 (8,126,464). s80
  crosses 0 at 8,388,608 (+0.07) and reaches the same loop state late (G
  +0.27, c0 +0.49, command 0.64 at 12M; its ledger at the PARK line at
  11.80M) with no tail over-bar episode, so no loop-only rule separates the
  runs with any margin (the best, G > 0.311, by 0.002 and 0.04) (EW
  `NOTES.txt` l14-72; VEW).
- **What training already records is as early or earlier**: the policy
  probe's `tail_3` offset on its bank's fixed states, and the ledger's tail
  command (CMD_L ≥ 0.3: 0.79M ahead in s42, 1.70M in s60) (EW).
- **The probe offset fails out of sample** (the ledger's tail command
  could not be tested: the r9 ledgers have no such column). On the five r9
  runs, with the rules hashed first, the probe bank's `tail_3` offset
  fired in all 5 by 2.06M, the cleanest negative (s70) included; the
  verifier calls it evidence against that signal as a cross-run warning
  (WR9 `wr9_r9.txt`; VWF; RUNLOG l773).
- **How close to the rail (WM, pre-registered).** The smallest constant
  nudge to the `tail_3` command's bias that parks it at the rail: s42 1.094
  (2.10M) and 0.281 (3.15M); s60 0.703 (6.29M) and 0.203 (7.34M); s80 0.516,
  0.328 and 0.297 (10.5M, 11.5M, 12.0M). Late s80 is as close as s42 one
  checkpoint before its first over-bar episode, but approaches 4-12x more
  slowly (s42 lost 0.81 of margin per 1.05M steps before it, s60 0.50; s80
  0.19, then 0.07). The gain-aware margin 1 - G - c0 ranks all seven
  checkpoints (Spearman +1.000); 1 - the command at the railed angle fits
  the scale best (mean absolute error 0.065) but swaps one pair (Spearman
  0.964); none rails on its own (G_cl ≤ 0.43) (WM `NOTES.txt` l9-84; VWF).
- **A correction to §1–§8 [L174].** §4.7, §4.11 and §7 item 1 give VPOST's
  G for r10 s42 as -0.250 at 1,048,576 and -0.386 at 2,097,152, and §2 the
  first. Those come from the 0-6° observation sweep, which at those
  checkpoints reaches the normaliser's clip beyond 3.74° and 4.28°. On the
  unclipped 0-1° sweep they are -0.725 and -0.636 (EW `NOTES.txt` l234-245;
  VEW `vew_clip.txt`). The signs hold, so §2's and §4.11's reading stands (a
  negative loop at 1,048,576); the magnitudes are larger. The figures above
  §9 are left as written.

### 9.4 The first critic-blind screen (BS): not informative

[measured, BS `score/score.txt` and `check/`, RUNLOG l772-l783; L177-L180]

- **Design** (the session's choice under the overnight authorisation, pinned
  before launch): 8 CPU continuations of s42 from 1,048,576, before its tail
  rose, to 2,490,368 (175 updates): 4 control and 4 `critic_blind_tail3`
  (the normalised `tail_3` angle set to 0 at the critic's input only; the
  actor still sees it), paired seeds 2101 to 2131. The primary is the
  policy's own `tail_3` command over the last 8 updates; an exact one-sided
  permutation test (smallest p 1/70) reads it only if the control median
  rises by at least 0.10 from the start (B_S -0.03157). The first draft of
  the criteria and scorer was reviewed independently (9 findings, none
  blocking) and fixed; the fixed version was not re-reviewed. APPROVED at
  06:00:05 UTC, 14 s before the launch.
- **Reading, final: line 1, "NOT INFORMATIVE"** (09:03 UTC). The control
  median rose +0.09184 (+0.06027 at the end), short of 0.10, so no reading
  about the critic is made. Primary, control against blind (seeds 2101,
  2111, 2121, 2131): -0.03102 / +0.02868, +0.02567 / -0.02845, +0.09486 /
  +0.03689, +0.28273 / +0.01563. Report only, and not evidence: arm means
  +0.09306 and +0.01319, p 13/70, sign-flip 4/16. All 8 COUNTED; the
  manipulation held on all 175 rollouts of every continuation. Both
  independent checks agree with no blocking finding; with the upper middle
  value in place of the pinned midpoint rule the gate would pass, but the
  line would read 3, not REDUCED, since p is 13/70 either way.
- **What it showed** (report only): from the same 1.05M networks the early
  rise is a matter of chance under fresh rollout seeds. Only control_2131
  rose like the parent on the primary (+0.314 against +0.231) and the logged
  tail command (0.32 against 0.255); control_2121 rose partway on the
  primary (+0.126) and tracked the parent on the statue_settled offset
  (+0.208 against +0.217), which control_2131 did not (+0.121) (the
  statistic lens's correction, STAT-1). All 8 were still far from the rail
  at 2.49M. The blinded runs' offsets c0 stayed at most 0.09, yet one
  blinded run's loop gain turned positive (+0.32); the end-G pattern (less
  negative than at the start in 4 of 4 blinded runs, 1 of 4 controls) was
  spotted after the fact and is untested (STAT-3).
- **Its text outputs** are on the orphan branch
  `claude/handoff-bias-screen` at `cfed2c70` (633 files, about 20 MB), pushed
  at 09:17 UTC under the authorisation and never to be merged (RUNLOG l782).

### 9.5 The second critic-blind screen (BS2): reduced

[measured, BS2 `score/score.txt`, `check/` and `REPORT.md`, RUNLOG
l784-l797; L181-L185]

- **Design** (the maintainer's option A, launched with its 0.20 gate): 8 CPU
  continuations of s42 from 2,097,152, mid-rise, where the probe verified the
  critic as the push's source, to 3,538,944 (175 updates): 4 control and 4
  `critic_blind_tail3`, paired seeds 2501 to 2531. The same primary and test;
  the gate a control median rise of at least 0.20 from B_S +0.12660, about a
  third of s42's own rise over the window (+0.57878); three secondaries that
  never change the line. The criteria and tools were built, checked,
  reviewed by a design and a process lens, fixed, and the fixed version
  re-reviewed READY TO PIN; APPROVED at 12:53:50 UTC.
- **Reading, final: line 2, "REDUCED: BLINDING THE CRITIC TO THE TAIL_3
  ANGLE (observation 6) REDUCED THE ON-POLICY TAIL_3 COMMAND OVER THE LAST 8
  UPDATES BEFORE E, FROM s42's 2,097,152 STATE (exact one-sided permutation
  test, p <= 0.05)"** (15:57 UTC). Primary, control against blind (seeds
  2501, 2511, 2521, 2531): +0.67001 / +0.14283, +0.79227 / +0.12485, +0.51081
  / +0.13810, +0.41938 / +0.06516. The control median rose +0.46382 (to
  +0.59041), past the 0.20 gate. Every control ended above every blinded
  run; p 1/70, the smallest attainable with 4 per arm.
- **Secondaries** (pre-registered; they never change the line): the
  ledger's tail command at the end SUPPORTED (p 1/70; control +0.884,
  +0.955, +0.597, +0.475, blind +0.181, +0.125, +0.146, +0.068); the margin
  to the rail at the end SUPPORTED (p 1/70; control -0.030, -0.060, +0.404,
  +0.352, blind +0.983, +1.109, +1.301, +1.194); both survive Bonferroni.
  The end loop gain, BS's after-the-fact pattern tested out of sample, NOT
  SUPPORTED (70/70: the blinded runs' gain was lower than the controls');
  controls that climb work against it, so it does not refute BS's pattern at
  1.05-2.49M (S-6).
- **Report only, with the checkers' wording** (S-2 to S-7, which
  `REPORT.md` applies): two of the four controls reached the bar and no
  blinded run did (control_2501 1 of 10 evaluation episodes over the bar at
  3,407,872, 0 at the end; control_2511 2 of 10 there and 8 of 10 at the
  end, a tail-flagged evaluation, all on `tail_3`; s42 itself first went
  over at 3,670,016, 4 of 10). The control median stayed within 0.03 of
  s42's own command through 3,145,728 and ended 0.115 below it (+0.590
  against +0.705), a median rise of 80% of s42's. The blinded runs' offsets
  c0 were 0.10-0.25 at the end (0.08-0.25 at every saved checkpoint) and
  their margin to the rail 0.98-1.30 (at least 0.95 throughout). The
  blinded runs' window return was higher in all 4 pairs (+6 to +34). The
  arms' spreads differ (SD 0.166 against 0.036): if blinding had only
  narrowed the spread, with no shift in the mean, a separation like this
  would occur about 3% of the time at this spread ratio (2.5-4% for ratios 3
  to 7; normal model), not 1.4%; the pre-registered p decides, and the line
  stands (post hoc Welch t 5.67, one-sided p 0.004).
- **Both independent checks agree** (104 comparisons with `score.json`, 0
  differences; the pins, APPROVED's timing 46 s before the first start, the
  critic exactly blind on every blinded rollout, outcome-blindness, as far
  as the access-time records can show (they cannot see a read before each
  decision, I1), and the limits all hold).
- **What it means** [measured, BS2 `REPORT.md` l60-73]: from s42's 2.10M
  state, removing the critic's view of the `tail_3` angle stopped the climb
  of the policy's tail command toward the rail; with the view, all four
  controls climbed. [inferred there] This fits the verified probe (§9.2): the
  critic's bootstrapped value pays for a raised tail, the policy gradient
  follows it, and the loop of §4.7 closes. It is the most direct causal
  evidence so far for the critic as the driver of the climb [L172].
- **Not shown** (BS2 `REPORT.md`): that it works from other runs or states
  (only s42 at 2.10M was tested); that a full training run with the critic
  blinded from the start avoids the habit and keeps a good stance; what the
  onset at about 1.2M does; whether the critic would in time recover the
  angle from its other inputs; whether the habit moves to another joint (s80's
  habit was on the neck).
- **Its text outputs** are on the orphan branch
  `claude/handoff-bias-screen2` at `2d241410` (3,220 files, about 47 MB),
  pushed at 16:26 UTC at the maintainer's word and never to be merged
  (RUNLOG l800).

### 9.6 What is answered, and what stays open (§7 updated)

**Answered or narrowed since §7** (each within the scope tested)

- §7 open question 1, why the loop forms in training: the policy-gradient
  term alone moves the tail mean, and from mid-rise the push comes through
  the critic's bootstrapped value [L171]; in s42 from 2,097,152, hiding the
  `tail_3` angle from the critic stopped the climb [L184, L185]. H-CRITIC is
  supported from mid-rise [L172].
- §7's "what changed at about 1.2M": at 1.31M s42 already had a push carried
  by its own exploration noise [L171]; whether the critic drives it is
  unresolved.
- §2's "a learned positive-feedback loop": it stands, and early warning from
  the loop alone does not separate the runs that rail from those that park
  late [L173, L176].
- From the 1,048,576 state the early rise is a matter of chance under fresh
  rollout seeds [L180].

**Still open**

- Why the critic values a raised tail (the tail angle as an episode-phase
  proxy is a hypothesis) [L171, L172].
- What drives the very first push at the onset (about 1.2M) [L171]; BS could
  not test it [L179].
- Whether the BS2 effect lasts to 12M, whether the stance gate still passes
  with the critic blinded, and whether the habit moves to another joint
  (s80's habit was on the neck): the questions of the long study, §9.7
  [L186].
- Whether the effect holds in other runs and states, and in a run blinded
  from the start; whether the critic would recover the angle from its other
  inputs [L185].
- An early warning that works across runs: none of the signals tested does
  [L173, L175, L176].
- §7's questions 2 to 17 stand as written, but that item 1's G values at
  1,048,576 and 2,097,152 are the clipped ones (§9.3) [L174], and the
  candidate next steps listed there are now behind the long study, which
  the maintainer chose (§9.7).

### 9.7 Next: the long critic-blind study (LB), chosen and pending

At 16:23 UTC the maintainer chose "Long blind run to 12M (Recommended)" (D-D58
(1); RUNLOG l798) [L186]: continue s42 from 2,097,152 steps to the full 12M, 4
runs, mostly `critic_blind_tail3` with a normal control, about 10 h on the
session's CPUs after a reviewed pre-registration of about 2 h, to ask whether
the effect lasts, whether the stance gate still passes, and whether the habit
moves to another joint. Its build and criteria were started at 16:32 UTC (a
harness that resumes from the latest saved pair, the round's pinned
measurements of the tail class, the alarms per actuator and the six-heading
gate, a draft of four questions with a default of 3 blinded and 1 control on
new seeds, then a build check, two reviews, a fixer and a re-review); as of
then, where this update's run-log copy ends, its pin and launch waited for the
maintainer and its result was pending (RUNLOG l801); what followed goes to the
next records change. It would train scratch continuations only; nothing trains
on `main`, and no gate, reward, plant or criterion changes.

### 9.8 Lessons

[inferred, L187] The record's §6 lessons stand; the update adds these
(§6.2's numbering continued):

20. A screen needs a control arm that reliably shows the effect. From 1.05M
    the rise came by chance under fresh seeds, BS's gate was not met, and it
    could say nothing; from the mid-rise start, where the probe had verified
    the critic's push, the controls rose and the test read.
21. A pre-registered informativeness gate kept an uninformative screen from
    being read as a null.
22. With 4 per arm the smallest p is 1/70; a reading at that bound needs the
    spread caveat beside it (BS2's S-3).
23. Re-review the fixed criteria, not only the first draft (BS's process
    check P2; done for BS2).
24. A sweep can reach the normaliser's clip; give a loop gain with its sweep
    range (§9.3).
25. An agent run as root can damage what every process shares: at about
    01:05 UTC one replaced the system's `/dev/null` with a regular file for
    1-2 minutes (repaired at 01:06 UTC; the probes' outputs unaffected; EW
    `NOTES.txt` l269-285, MR §5). Each agent writes only inside its own
    folder.
26. A durable text copy of each screen, made after its reading and never
    merged, keeps what the repository cannot hold.

### 9.9 This update's sources and files
